### Finding: Go has no Multi-Agent V2 catalog tool-message surface

Rust's `ModelMessages` resolves per-tool overrides through
`prompts::model_messages::multi_agent_tool_*`: `messages.tools.multi_agent`
holds one `ToolMessage` per Multi-Agent V2 tool (`spawn_agent`, `send_message`,
`followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, `list_agents`), each with an
optional `description` and - since #46505 - an optional JSON-encoded
`parameters` schema. `core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs` passes both overrides to
`multi_agent_v2_handler`, which validates the schema (JSON object with
`type: "object"` in the harness's supported subset, failing when a bundled
`encrypted: true` property is missing from the override) and otherwise logs and
falls back to the bundled parameters; descriptions apply to the five tools other
than `spawn_agent`, whose description is composed with runtime guidance.

Go models none of this:

- `model.ToolMessages` carries only `send_user_message_async`, and
  `model.ToolMessage` has no `parameters` field, so a catalog's multi-agent tool
  messages are dropped on parse.
- `agent/tools_v2.go` hardcodes every V2 tool's description and
  `InputSchema`/`OutputSchema` in `multiAgentV2ToolExecutor.Spec()` with no
  override application (the executor already carries the model-derived
  `usageHintText`/`hideSpawnMetadata`/`exposeSpawnModelOverrides` inputs, so the
  catalog messages are the missing input).

Next step for this lane: add the catalog types and accessors
(`ToolMessages.MultiAgent`, `ToolMessage.Parameters`,
`MultiAgentToolMessage(tool)`), then apply the description override to the five
tools and the validated parameter override to all six inside `Spec()`, with the
bundled-schema fallback and the encrypted-property rule, plus tests mirroring
`core/tests/suite/multi_agent_tool_descriptions.rs`.

The gateway-OAuth chain recorded in sync41 remains the other core lane and is
still the larger one (it needs the `login/src/oauth/*` + `gateway_auth*` runtime
before #46490's composition can land).

## sync42 continuation (same upstream head `dfb265764d`)

### The Multi-Agent V2 catalog tool-message surface (#46505), and the finding above closed

The Go gap recorded above is closed. Rust's catalog now supplies per-tool V2
descriptions and JSON-encoded parameter schemas through `tools.multi_agent.<tool>`,
validated and applied by `multi_agent_v2_handler`; Go modelled none of it.

- `model.ToolMessage` gained `parameters`, `ToolMessages` gained `multi_agent`,
  and `MultiAgentToolMessages` carries the six tools. `ModelMessages` exposes
  `MultiAgentToolMessage` / `MultiAgentToolDescriptionOverride` /
  `MultiAgentToolParametersOverride`, resolving by tool name independently of the
  namespace, and the model-info clone now deep-copies the tool messages.
- `agent.MultiAgentToolOverride` travels from `MultiAgentHandlerOptions.ToolOverrides`
  into `multiAgentV2ToolExecutor.Spec()`: the catalog description replaces the
  bundled text for every V2 tool except `spawn_agent` (whose description also
  composes runtime usage guidance), and the parameter override is used only when
  it decodes as JSON, declares an object root, uses the supported schema subset,
  and still declares every bundled encrypted property (whose marker the harness
  restores). Anything else keeps the bundled parameters, matching Rust's
  warn-and-fallback.
- `jsonschema.SubsetSchema` provides the validating half of the subset contract
  (serde into Rust's `JsonSchema`: unknown keys dropped, malformed known keys
  rejected), and the shared subset key list gained `minItems`, which Rust's
  `JsonSchema` carries but Go had been dropping.
- Plumbing: `turn.MultiAgentToolOverridesFromCatalog` builds the per-tool map
  from the active model's messages, the app-server turn fills it from
  `turnModelInfo.ModelMessages`, and the exec path fills it from the models
  manager's catalog for the run's model.

Evidence: `model.TestModelMessagesMultiAgentToolOverridesLikeRust` (selection by
name, an explicit empty description preserved, absent entries nil, and the clone
no longer sharing the override pointers),
`turn.TestMultiAgentToolOverridesFromCatalogMatchesRust`,
`agent.TestMultiAgentV2CatalogOverridesApplyLikeRust` (description + schema
replacement and the restored encryption marker),
`agent.TestMultiAgentV2SpawnKeepsComposedDescriptionLikeRust` (spawn keeps its
composed text and usage hint while its schema override applies),
`agent.TestMultiAgentV2InvalidCatalogOverridesFallBackLikeRust` (invalid JSON,
non-object root, non-object type, unsupported structure, unsupported nested
structure, and a dropped bundled encrypted property all fall back; a
description-only override leaves the parameters alone),
`agent.TestMultiAgentV2UnknownCatalogEntriesAreIgnoredLikeRust`, and
`jsonschema.TestSubsetSchemaMatchesRustSubset` /
`TestSubsetSchemaRejectsUnsupportedStructures` (unknown keys dropped, `minItems`
kept, malformed known keys rejected, input not mutated). Verification:
`go test ./... -count=1` exit 0.

### Metadata-only running-resume snapshots (#46510)

Rust stopped cloning a running thread's active-turn items for resumes that do
not need them: `needs_items = include_turns || (initial page requested with an
items view other than NotLoaded)`, with a metadata-only turn snapshot used for
the status check and the NotLoaded page.

- `RuntimeRouter.activeRuntimeTurnSnapshotMetadata` returns the running turn with
  `TurnItemsNotLoaded` and no items (sharing one builder with the full snapshot,
  which is now only used when items are needed), and
  `applyRunningThreadResumeSnapshot` gates the full snapshot on that rule.

Evidence: `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterRunningResumeTurnItemsViewLikeRust` (a
running thread resumed with `excludeTurns` and a NotLoaded page reports the
active turn with no items, Summary/Full pages carry the requested view and the
turn's items, and the metadata builder never materializes items while the full
builder does), mirroring Rust's extended running-paginated-resume test.
Verification: `go test ./... -count=1` exit 0. The static parity layer needed no
re-pin for this commit (it changed no pinned fixture root, hash or snapshot).

### The catalog follows the current credentials before turns and wakeups (#46508)

Rust refreshes a catalog that belongs to other credentials before a user turn and
before a mailbox-triggered wakeup, then rechecks the active turn after discovery;
static catalogs need no refresh, and a failure or the five-second deadline leaves
the existing cache or bundled fallback.

- `model.ModelsManager` gained `RefreshAfterAuthChange` and `CatalogIdentity`
  (Rust's endpoint-client identity): the static manager is a no-op with an empty
  identity, the lazy manager forwards both, and `RemoteModelsManager` compares
  the identity the in-memory catalog was fetched under (`fetchedIdentity`, now
  recorded on fetch and cache load) with the configured identity and refreshes
  with `OnlineIfUncached` under the same five-second deadline when they differ.
  `shouldRefreshModels` mirrors Rust's gate (Codex-backend catalog, command auth,
  or API-key discovery).
- `RuntimeRouter.refreshModelCatalogAfterAuthChange` resolves the identity the
  running configuration would use (`accountScopedCatalogIdentity`, extracted
  with `resolveAccountScopedCatalogProvider` so the manager build and the
  identity cannot drift) and, when it differs from the running catalog's, rebuilds
  the account-scoped manager and claims the new catalog before use. Go's manager
  captures its identity and endpoint auth when built while Rust's endpoint client
  reads the live auth manager, so rebuilding under the current credentials is Go's
  equivalent of Rust's in-place refresh; an unresolvable identity on either side
  leaves the running manager to its own claim check, and a failed rebuild keeps
  the current catalog.
- The refresh runs before a user turn (`runTurnRuntime`) and before a
  queued-submission wakeup (`maybeDispatchQueuedSubmissionIfIdle`), where the
  wakeup rechecks the active turn before dispatching, matching Rust's ordering.

Evidence: `model.TestRemoteModelsManagerRefreshAfterAuthChangeMatchesRust` (an
unclaimed catalog is fetched under the current identity, a matching catalog is
left alone, a host whose catalog credentials cannot change never refreshes, and a
discovery failure leaves the fallback), `appserver.
TestRuntimeRouterRefreshesCatalogForCurrentCredentialsLikeRust` (a real
account-scoped manager over a local models endpoint: an unchanged credential
leaves the catalog in place, a rotated account rebuilds it and fetches it under
the new identity, and the refreshed catalog then matches), plus the manager
identity accessors exercised by the existing catalog tests. Verification:
`go test ./... -count=1` exit 0 and `go test ./parity/ -count=1` exit 0.

Recorded nuance: Rust refreshes the same manager in place; Go rebuilds the
account-scoped manager, because Go's models endpoint captures its auth headers
and identity at construction rather than reading a live auth manager. The
observable outcome for a turn is the same (the catalog and its requests use the
current credentials), at the cost of re-resolving the provider and auth on a
credential change.

### ChatGPT cookies are shared with secure WebSocket handshakes (#46506)

Rust made WebSocket handshakes reuse the HTTP factory's ChatGPT cookie store and
retain allowlisted infrastructure cookies from the response, so routing cookies
such as `__oailb` survive into later connections. Go kept an equivalent jar but
only wired it into the exec-server HTTP client, and its scope check rejected
`wss` targets.

- The jar moved from `execserver` to `network` (`network/chatgpt_cookies.go`,
  `SharedChatGPTCookieJar`), keeping the allowlist, the ChatGPT-host rule and
  the HTTPS scope, and gained `ChatGPTCookieHeader` / `StoreChatGPTResponseCookies`.
  `chatGPTCookieURL` maps a `wss` target to `https` before the scope check
  (Rust `chatgpt_cookie_url`: a secure handshake has the same scope as HTTPS),
  so insecure `ws` targets and non-ChatGPT hosts still have no shared cookies.
- The Responses WebSocket handshake (`model/responses_agent.go`) adds the shared
  cookie header only when the handshake has no explicit `Cookie` header, and
  stores the response's infrastructure cookies for both successful and rejected
  upgrades (Rust `connect_with_route`).
- `execserver/custom_ca.go` now uses the moved jar, so the HTTP transports keep
  the same store.

Evidence: `network.TestChatGPTCookieScopeMatchesRust` (HTTPS and WSS share the
scope, `ws` and non-ChatGPT hosts do not, and the jar itself rejects
non-ChatGPT targets), `network.TestChatGPTResponseCookiesOnlyRetainInfrastructureCookies`
(only `__oailb`/`__cf_bm` are retained from a mixed `Set-Cookie` response,
account/session cookies are excluded, deletion removes a retained cookie, and
non-ChatGPT responses store nothing),
`network.TestSharedChatGPTCookieHeaderReachesTheSharedStore` (a rejected
upgrade's refreshed cookie is visible to the next handshake header), the moved
`network.TestChatGPTCloudflareCookieStore*` cases (allowlist, `__oailb` scope
replay and deletion, HTTP/suffix-trick rejection), and
`model.TestResponsesAgentRunnerWebsocketKeepsExplicitCookieHeaderLikeRust` (an
explicit provider `Cookie` header is not overridden). Verification:
`go test ./... -count=1` exit 0.

Recorded nuance: Rust marks the generated cookie header sensitive; Go's
`http.Header` has no such flag, and the value is only sent over TLS. The
model-level "shared cookie reaches the handshake" path is covered at the helper
level rather than end to end, because the jar's host rule requires a ChatGPT
hostname while Go's WebSocket dial has no injectable dialer/custom host mapping.

### The usage-tag diagnostics on the sampling span (#46501)

Rust replaced the sampling span's `features` field with a `tags_json` document: a
searchable, scalar-only allowlist of usage settings (configured vs resolved
context window, compaction and token limits, verbosity, sub-agent defaults,
multi-agent v2, memories, token/rollout budgets, service tier) plus one boolean
tag per reported feature, excluding Collab and SpawnCsv. Go's sampling span
carried only `turn_id`/`model`/`cwd`.

- `appserver.UsageTagsForTurn` mirrors `core::feedback_config::usage_tags` over
  the same allowlist (absent or null renders as the explicit `unset` marker,
  strings stay verbatim, scalars print canonically) and adds `feature.<key>` for
  every registry feature except `multi_agent` and `enable_fanout`.
  `appserver.UsageTagsJSON` renders the document with sorted keys (Rust's span
  field shows the serialized map).
- `appTurnRunConfig.UsageTags` is computed once per turn in `appTurnConfig`
  (which has the effective config, resolved model info, and service tier) and
  passed into both `turn.AgentLoopRequest` sites; the sampling-request span adds
  `tags_json` when the turn has tags.
- `model.ModelInfo.UsableContextWindow` exports the existing resolved-window
  calculation so the allowlist reports the same effective window Rust does.

Evidence: `appserver.TestUsageTagsConfiguredAndEffectiveContextAreDistinctLikeRust`
(Rust's `configured_and_effective_context_are_distinct_and_unset_is_explicit`:
`872000`/`true`/`380000`/`priority`, then `unset`/`false`/`258400`/`unset`),
`appserver.TestUsageTagsReportEveryFeatureExceptCollabAndSpawnCsvLikeRust`
(every registry feature toggles false/true except the two exclusions),
`appserver.TestUsageTagsDocumentJSONLikeRust`, and
`turn.TestAgentLoopSamplingSpanCarriesUsageTagsLikeRust` (the span carries the
sorted document). Verification: `go test ./... -count=1` exit 0.

Recorded nuance: Rust's `usage_tags` takes a `Features` object, which the
collector can also mutate directly; Go derives the per-feature tags from a
settings map (`cfg.FeatureSettings()` in production), so the collector half of
the Rust change — scraping `tags_json` out of tracing and expanding it into
feedback upload tags with `MAX_FEEDBACK_TAGS` raised to 512 — has no Go
counterpart: Go's feedback module takes caller-supplied tags rather than reading
the tracing pipeline.

### Next step for the #46499 remainder, scoped while closing #46501

The unproxied-escalation half needs its implementation site pinned first, and
this pass established where it is not: in the unified-exec path
(`tool/shell_executor.go`'s `Execute`) the managed-network resolver runs *before*
`BuildShellRequest`, so the resolved `ShellRequest.SandboxPermissions` (which the
approval flow may have downgraded) is not available at the resolver block, and
`ShellValidationOptions` carries no permission field at all. Either resolve the
attempt's permissions earlier (`args.SandboxPermissions` through
`sandbox.SandboxPermissionsPreservingDeniedReads`, mirroring Rust's
`SandboxPermissionsPreservingDeniedReads`) or move the resolver after
`BuildShellRequest`; when the resolved value still requires escalation, skip
`validation.Env`/`ManagedNetwork`/`RemoteNetworkProxy`/decider attachment while
recording `EnforceManagedNetwork` so the retained terminal reports the bypass
(Rust `tools/runtimes/unified_exec.rs` + `tools/orchestrator.rs`
`managed_network_active`). Rust's `ESCALATED_DENY_READ` case is the regression
guard: a downgraded escalation must keep the proxy so denied reads stay enforced.

## sync43: upstream 36430b3688..22dea11020 (22 commits)

Fast-forwarded the local Rust checkout from the sync42 head `36430b3688`
(#46509) to `22dea11020` (#46535) and triaged the 22 new commits. The static
parity layer pinpoints the range: `go test ./parity/` failed at the new
checkout with four explicit drift items before any code changed, and
`./appserver/` + `./cli/` were already green. Most of the range is Rust-internal
(test stabilisation, crate renames, Guardian/mount refactors), so the
dispositions below separate Go-visible behaviour from Rust-only structure.

### Landed in this pass

#### 1. Static parity alignment (the four drift items, closed)

- `model.ModelInfo` gained `supports_reasoning_effort_updates`
  (`supports_reasoning_effort_updates`, serde default false), carried through the
  raw catalog decode; the L0 field-surface pins moved 45->46 (Rust) and
  47->48 (Go). Evidence: `parity.TestRustModelInfoFieldSurfaceAgainstGo`.
- The `guardian_reuse_parent_compaction` feature was promoted to `StageStable`
  with `DefaultEnabled: true` (#46522). Evidence:
  `parity.TestFeaturesRegistryMatchesRust`,
  `parity.TestRustFeatureKeySurfaceAgainstGo`.
- The `core/src/client.rs` critical-file hash was re-pinned to the new
  reasoning-effort override gate (#46530/#46531). Evidence:
  `parity.TestRustCriticalFileHashesSnapshot`.
- The TUI snapshot manifest total moved 1168->1171 and the `tui/tests/suite/snapshots`
  entry 1->4 for the three new `daemon_compatibility__*` snapshots. Evidence:
  `parity.TestRustTUISnapshotManifestCoversPrioritySurfaces`.

#### 2. Reasoning-effort updates require explicit model support (#46530)

Rust replaced the Responses-Lite-based gate with `ModelInfo.supports_reasoning_effort_updates`,
required an OpenAI provider, and cleared the pinned baseline when sampling with
an unsupported model. Saved `configuration_update` items are now filtered out of
the request copy for unsupported models.

- `reasoningoverride.OverrideEnabled(featureEnabled, providerIsOpenAI, info)`
  is the combined gate; `EffortForConfigurationUpdate` now takes that decision
  instead of re-deriving it; `RequestEffort` clears the pin on sampling whenever
  the combined gate is off.
- `model.AgentRequest.DropReasoningEffortUpdates` (threaded through
  `turn.AgentLoopRequest`) drops `configuration_update` items from the request
  input; the app-server and exec entry points set it from the combined gate.
  Persisted history is unchanged.

Evidence: `appserver.TestEffortForConfigurationUpdateGatingLikeRust`
(support independent of Responses Lite, non-OpenAI rejected),
`exec.TestExecReasoningEffortOverrideGatingLikeRust` (the gate result is
reported), `model.TestResponsesInputItemsDropUnsupportedConfigurationUpdatesLikeRust`
(filtered request copy, untouched supported path), and the reworked exec
end-to-end test that now injects a `model_catalog_json` whose entry sets the
flag. Verification: `go test ./... -count=1` exit 0.

#### 3. Fixed-effort workers keep request effort (#46531)

Rust exempts memory-consolidation sessions (current and legacy) and ephemeral
`thread_title` threads from reasoning-effort overrides; persisted `thread_title`
threads and other ephemeral threads keep the override.

- `RuntimeRouter.reasoningEffortOverrideExempt` reads the thread's stored
  source/thread-source and ephemeral marker; `reasoningEffortOverrideEnabled`
  folds it together with the feature, provider, and model support.

Evidence: `appserver.TestReasoningEffortOverrideExemptsFixedEffortWorkersLikeRust`
(memory and ephemeral title exempt; persisted title and ordinary ephemeral
threads not exempt).

#### 4. New TUI threads disable reasoning summaries by default (#46533)

- `interactiveEmbeddedReasoningOverrides` now defaults
  `model_reasoning_summary` to `none` and keeps concurrent summaries off unless
  summaries are explicitly configured and enabled; enabling
  `concurrent_reasoning_summaries` alone no longer turns summaries on.

Evidence: `app.TestInteractiveEmbeddedReasoningOverridesLikeRust` (default off,
concurrent-only off, explicit `detailed` preserved, `concise` + concurrent stays
on).

### Recorded as Rust-only (no Go counterpart)

- #46521/#46524/#46519/#46516/#46517/#46518/#46514: test stabilisation and
  helper refactors around Rust process groups, Tokio paused time, and the
  packaged-daemon tests.
- #46510/#46511: perf refactors that avoid cloning turn items during thread
  resume; the Go resume path already filters excluded items and does not clone
  active turn items the same way.
- #46513: the Rust-internal `AgentControl` -> `LocalAgentControl` rename.
- #46532: removing `com.apple.runningboard` from the Seatbelt read-only platform
  defaults file. Go's Seatbelt profile builder (`sandbox/seatbelt.go`) does not
  embed `sandboxing/src/seatbelt_read_only_platform_defaults.sbpl`, so there is
  no Go surface for the entry.
- #46535: Linux sandbox namespace-mount validation in
  `linux-sandbox/src/daemon_mounts.rs`; Go's `sandbox/linuxsandbox` does not
  implement that mount-table socket-isolation check.
- #46527: WinGet dependency pinning in the release workflow.
- #46534: `env_inherit` support in a Rust workspace-root test.

#### 5. Plugin measurements bind to the matched trusted root version (#46528)

- `trustedPluginRoot` now carries the installed version, and
  `TrustedPluginRoots.matchedScript` returns the single root (plus its manifest
  declaration) that owns the canonical script. `Resolve` and
  `ResolveMetricsOperation` both derive from it, so a declaration can no longer
  be supplied by a different version of the same plugin that happens to share a
  relative path.

Evidence: `plugin.TestResolveMetricsOperationBindsToMatchedTrustedRootVersion`
(the older version's declaration applies to its own script, the newer version
does not inherit it, and attribution still spans versions). Verification:
`go test ./plugin/ -count=1` exit 0.

### Remaining lanes for the next pass

- #46523 (default local binding to true for MXC managed networking): no
  Go-visible change while Go has no native MXC runner. `execserver` rejects the
  MXC sandbox level outright (`TestExecServerRejectsMXCLevelLikeRust`), so the
  `RequireTrue` policy default and the "MXC cannot enforce
  `allow_local_binding=false`" rejection have no executor to act on. Go's
  non-MXC default (`false`) already matches Rust's `DefaultFalse` policy.
- #46529 (compatible feature overrides when starting the shared daemon): the Go
  TUI never auto-starts the shared daemon. The `daemon_auto_start` feature key
  exists in the registry but has no consumer, and `app/interactive.go` always
  runs the embedded app server, so Rust's override-eligibility list,
  launch-feature persistence, and `experimentalFeature/list` compatibility probe
  have no Go startup path to attach to.
- #46512 (Guardian review checkpoints from live context): Go's Guardian review
  session already seeds later reviews from in-memory state
  (`ResetAfterParentCompaction` / `SeedForReview` in
  `appserver/guardian_reviewer.go`); there is no storage-based fork-history load
  to replace, so the Rust refactor has no Go counterpart.
- The sync41 plan's #46499 remainder is down to item 2: the launch baseline that
  records a bypassed launch (`tools/orchestrator.rs` computes
  `managed_network_active` even when escalation skips the execution proxy). Go's
  `EnforceManagedNetwork` means "a proxy was attached" and is consumed as
  `ProxyEnforced` by the sandbox launch, so it must stay false for escalated
  attempts; the Rust baseline's only consumer here is the terminal review, and
  Go's review already reaches the same decision without it:
  `RuntimeRouter.terminalWriteReviewRequirement` returns `RequireEscalated` for a
  bypassed launch (`request.SandboxPermissions == require_escalated`) and for any
  profile drift, including a changed environment network policy. Item 2 is
  therefore recorded as no remaining Go-visible delta rather than an open gap.

### sync43 continuation: catalog model display names in the TUI (#46503)

The sync42 triage queued this item. Go showed the raw model slug on every

- `tea.Model.modelDisplayName` resolves a model id through the TUI's

- `chatwidget.ModelPopupPreset` gained `DisplayName` (Rust

Evidence: `tea.TestModelDisplayNameSurfacesLikeRust` (display name in the
session header and status runtime, id fallback for unlisted models).
Verification: `go test ./tui/... ./app/... -count=1` exit 0.

Still queued: `907b751eab` six bundled TUI themes and theme-aware accents
(#46504).

### sync43 continuation 2: the six bundled TUI themes (#46504, first half)

Rust bundled `ada`, `babbage`, `curie`, `cushman`, `dali`, and `davinci` as
TextMate theme assets and shipped them through the theme picker.

- The six `.tmTheme` assets are vendored under `tui/themes/assets/` and embedded

  precedence), then the bundled assets, then the approximate family prefixes.
- `bundledThemeIDs` gained the six ids, so they appear in the theme picker.

  `#5FAFFF`), the input for Rust's theme-aware accent.

Evidence: `tui.TestBundledThemesLikeRust` (list membership, a registered style
per theme, and the declared accent with an empty fallback for unknown names).
Verification: `go test ./... -count=1` exit 0.

Still open in #46504: use `codex.accent` for active/selected controls on
truecolor and 256-colour terminals (Rust `style.rs::accent_style`), honour
terminal-default diff backgrounds and clear matching gutter fills (the `dali`
and `davinci` "no background fill" case in `diff_render.rs`), and restrict the
Windows native palette fallback to `ConsoleWindowClass`.

#### Remaining #46504 parts, scoped against the current Go surface

- **[landed]** **`codex.accent` for active/selected controls**: Rust's
  `style::accent_style()` funnel now returns the theme's `codex.accent` on
  truecolor/256-colour terminals and keeps `accent_style_for(default_bg())`
  below that. Go's accent-text funnel is `styles.Dialog.Highlight` (used by the
  async-question and read-only-thread prompts that mirror Rust's
  `accent_style()` call sites): `tui.ThemeAccentColor` resolves a custom
  `.tmTheme` accent first and the bundled asset second, `tui.ThemeAccentSGR`
  renders it as a bold SGR prefix (rgb at truecolor, `ClosestANSI256` index at
  256 colours, "" below), and `tea.applyThemeAccent` applies it to
  `Dialog.Highlight` when the model is constructed. `tea.statusLineAccentSGR`
  stays on its own Rust module (per-item accent-scope borrowing) and is not
  repainted from the single accent colour. Evidence:
  `tui.TestThemeAccentSGRRespectsColorDepthLikeRust` (`\x1b[1;38;2;95;175;255m`
  at truecolor, `\x1b[1;38;5;75m` at 256, the fallback below, and the fallback
  for a theme without an accent).
- **Terminal-default diff backgrounds**: Rust's `DiffScopeBackgrounds` +
  `DiffScopeBackground::TerminalDefault` (the six new themes express it as
  `#00000001`) sits on top of `diff_scope_background_rgbs`, which reads
  `markup.inserted`/`markup.deleted` background colours from the active syntax
  theme. Go has no theme-scope diff-background mechanism at all
  (`tui/diff_render.go` colours diffs from its hardcoded palette via
  `diffBgSGR`; `tmtheme.go` maps those scopes to foreground token colours only),
  so the sentinel has no Go site to alter.
- **Windows native palette fallback**: Rust restricts it to
  `ConsoleWindowClass`. Go has no native Windows console-palette probe
  (`tui/terminal_probe/*` has no Go counterpart; the Go `conpty` packages are for
  PTY spawning), so there is no fallback to restrict.

## sync44: upstream 22dea11020..a633ebc124 (13 commits)

Fast-forwarded the Rust checkout from the sync43 head `22dea11020` (#46535) to
`a633ebc124` (#46554). The static parity layer pinned the range again:
`go test ./parity/` failed with the config schema/ConfigToml counts, the
`cli/tests` and `core/tests/suite` fixture counts, the `core/src/client.rs`
hash, and the vendored precomputed app-server exports.

### Landed in this pass

#### 1. Static parity alignment

- The new top-level config key `model_post_turn_compact_threshold_percent` is
  recognised (schema 99->100, `ConfigToml` 102->103, Go keys 106->107) and
  joined to the nullable-key surface.
- `cli/tests` 29->31 (the doctor filesystem-path diagnostics snapshots) and
  `core/tests/suite` 211->212 (`command_lifecycle_tests.rs`, #46539).
- `core/src/client.rs` re-pinned to the retry-centralization commit (#46540).
- Precomputed app-server exports re-vendored from the new checkout and the
  pinned artifact hashes updated (#46547/#46553).

Evidence: `parity.TestRustConfigSchemaSurfaceAgainstGo`,
`parity.TestRustConfigTomlTopLevelSurfaceSnapshot`,
`parity.TestRustGoldenFixtureRootsSnapshot`,
`parity.TestRustCriticalFileHashesSnapshot`,
`parity.TestPrecomputedAppServerExportsMatchRustTarget`,
`appserver.TestPrecomputedExportArtifactsMatchTargetRustCommit`.

#### 2. Opt-in compaction after final responses (#46541)

Rust added `model_post_turn_compact_threshold_percent`: when a turn's final
response leaves context usage at or above that percentage of the usable window
(or an existing auto-compaction limit), the turn compacts before completing.

- `config.Config.ModelPostTurnCompactThresholdPercent` reads the value (omitted
  or zero disables) and config load rejects values above 100 with Rust's
  message.
- `compact.PhasePostTurn` and `telemetry.CompactionPhasePostTurn` carry the new
  analytics phase; the analytics mapping resolves it.
- `appserver.turnEndCompactionThresholdReached` mirrors Rust
  `ContextWindowTokenStatus::turn_end_compaction_threshold_reached`;
  `RuntimeRouter.postTurnCompact` runs the PostTurn compaction after the final
  response when the threshold is reached, the token-budget feature is off, no
  steer input is queued (`turn.SteerMailbox.HasPending`), and the turn was not
  cancelled. It takes precedence over the ordinary turn-end compaction so the
  history is summarized once; failures other than cancellation keep the
  completed turn, while cancellation ends it.

Evidence: `config.TestModelPostTurnCompactThresholdPercentLikeRust`,
`appserver.TestTurnEndCompactionThresholdReachedLikeRust`,
`appserver.TestRuntimeRouterPostTurnCompactLikeRust` (the compaction request
carries `PhasePostTurn` + `TriggerAuto` and the record's `compaction_phase` is
`postTurn`; zero disables it). Verification:
`go test ./appserver/ ./config/ ./compact/ ./turn/ ./telemetry/ ./exec/ -count=1`
exit 0.

#### 3. Sampling retry backoff matches Rust's shared helper (#46540)

Rust replaced `is_retryable()` with `CodexErr::retry_delay(retry_count)`: server
advice wins when present, otherwise `codex_async_utils::backoff` (200 ms doubling
per attempt, 0.9..1.1 jitter, no ceiling) and terminal error kinds never retry.

- `model.responsesRetryDelay` already preferred `Retry-After` (including a
  zero-duration advice) and used the same base/jitter, but carried a 5-second
  ceiling that Rust does not have. The ceiling is removed so later attempts wait
  the full doubling delay; the retry budget (`requestMaxRetries`, default 9)
  still bounds the loop, and the doubling saturates instead of overflowing.
- Terminal classification is unchanged: `shouldRetryResponsesHTTPRequest` still
  decides retryability, so server advice cannot make a terminal error retryable.

Evidence: `model.TestResponsesRetryDelayMatchesRustBackoff` (attempts 0/1 -> 200 ms,
2 -> 400 ms, 3 -> 800 ms, 6 -> 6.4 s within the jitter band and strictly above the
old 5 s ceiling). Verification: `go test ./model/ -count=1` exit 0.

| `b244e9bafe` command-start callback for tool lifecycle extensions (#46539) | **queued**: new tool lifecycle extension point |

### Verification note (sync44)

Package-level runs after the landed commits all pass:

and `go test ./appserver/ -count=1` (207 s) and `go test ./tool/ -count=1` (77 s).

Two full-suite (`go test ./...`) failures were load-sensitive timing tests that

## sync45: the removed Windows private-desktop setting (#46554)

Rust deleted the `windows.sandbox_private_desktop` opt-out (and its managed
requirement and API field) and now always launches legacy Windows sandboxes on a
private desktop, warning users to remove the obsolete key.

- `config.ConfigRequirements` lost `WindowsSandboxPrivateDesktop`, and the
  `[windows] sandbox_private_desktop`/legacy `permissions` spelling is no longer
  parsed into requirements (`requirements_file.go`), merged from cloud configs
  (`cloud_config.go`), cloned, or warned about as an overridden value
  (`startup_warnings.go`). `ResolveWindowsSandboxPrivateDesktop` and the
  appserver config helpers are gone; `windowsSandboxPrivateDesktopForTurn()`
  returns true for the shell-validation and executor-skill sandbox contexts.
- The obsolete key is now reported like an unrecognized setting:
  `config.obsoleteConfigFieldPaths` adds `windows.sandbox_private_desktop` (and
  the legacy `permissions.windows_sandbox_private_desktop`) to
  `IgnoredConfigWarning`, and `ignoredSettingHint` returns Rust's migration text
  (" Remove windows.sandbox_private_desktop; legacy Windows sandboxes always use
  a private desktop.").

Evidence: `config.TestWindowsSandboxPrivateDesktopSettingIsObsoleteLikeRust`
(the key no longer reaches requirements and both spellings produce the hint),
`config.TestParseRequirementsTOMLWindowsLikeRust` (a `[windows]` table carrying
the obsolete key still parses its implementation restriction),
`appserver.TestWindowsSandboxPrivateDesktopIsAlwaysEnabledLikeRust`, and the
updated `config.TestStartupWarningsForRequirementsLikeRust` (no override warning
for the removed field). Verification: `go test ./config/ ./parity/ ./exec/
-count=1` and `go test ./appserver/ -count=1` exit 0.

Recorded nuance: Go still carries a `WindowsSandboxPrivateDesktop` bool through
`tool`/`execserver`/`sandbox/windowssandbox` (always true from the appserver) and
the wrapper exposes `--windows-sandbox-private-desktop`. Rust now removes the
opt-out at those layers too and requires a private-desktop *name* at the wrapper
and command runner; aligning that needs the Windows desktop-name plumbing, which
is scoped separately.

## sync45 continuation: the declared onboarding skill (#46544)

Rust added an optional `onboardingSkill` summary to `plugin/read`: the manifest's
`extensions["com.openai"].onboardingSkill` is resolved against the plugin root
(relative, with or without the legacy `./`, and it must stay inside the root)
and matched against the plugin's skills, returning the summary only while the
plugin and that skill are enabled.

- `pluginManifestFile` captures `onboardingSkill` from the Agent Plugins
  extension (and the `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` overlay), and
  `resolveOnboardingSkillPath` resolves it with the plugin-root containment rule.
- `PluginDetail` carries the internal resolved path plus the exported
  `OnboardingSkill *PluginSkill` (`onboardingSkill`), filled by
  `onboardingSkillForDetail` at read time when the plugin and the matching skill
  are enabled; the detail merge keeps the declaration when the installed detail
  wins, and `cloneDetail`/`MarshalJSON` carry the value.

Evidence: `plugin.TestOnboardingSkillForDetailLikeRust` (enabled match, disabled
plugin, disabled skill, missing and unmatched declarations),
`plugin.TestResolveOnboardingSkillPathLikeRust` (`./` prefix accepted, escaping
and absolute declarations rejected), and
`plugin.TestPluginReadExposesDeclaredOnboardingSkillLikeRust` (end-to-end
plugin/read plus the serialized `onboardingSkill`).

Recorded nuance: Rust's *remote* variant matches the release metadata's
`onboarding_skill_name` and additionally requires the plugin to be available.
Go's remote catalog (`plugin.RemotePluginCatalog`) carries no release skill list,
so that branch has no Go input; an installed remote plugin's own bundled manifest
declaration is what applies instead.

Verification: `go test ./plugin/ ./parity/ -count=1` and
`go test ./appserver/ -count=1` exit 0. The appserver package run also flaked
twice on unrelated timing tests (`TestOtelProviderReloadsAfterAccountChange`,
`TestRuntimeRouterCommandApprovalExecPolicyAmendmentRunsAndEmitsReviewAnalyticsLikeRust`)
that pass in isolation and passed with the same change applied on a re-run; a
stashed (unchanged) run passed as well, so the flakiness is not attributable to
this change.

### Next step: `codex doctor` filesystem-path diagnostics (#46543)

The only remaining Go-visible item in the range. Rust's surface is mapped:
`sandbox.filesystem_paths` (category `sandbox`) reports literal permission-profile
grants with access mode and resolution outcome, excluding deny rules and never
expanding globs or special paths; resolution runs in disposable copies of the
executable via a hidden `doctor --probe-filesystem-path <path>` subcommand with
`PROBE_TIMEOUT` 2 s, `TOTAL_TIMEOUT` 8 s, `SLOW_PROBE` 1 s and `MAX_PATHS` 32;
Windows or read-restricted policies only list paths; missing paths alone do not
warn; and the app-server doctor report timeout moves from 25 s to 35 s. Go's
doctor already has a `sandboxCheck` and a check list in `doctor/doctor.go`, so
the port needs the new check plus the hidden CLI probe subcommand.

## sync45 continuation 2: `codex doctor` filesystem-path diagnostics (#46543)

Rust added `sandbox.filesystem_paths` to the doctor report: literal
permission-profile grants with their access modes and resolution outcome,
excluding deny rules and never expanding globs or special paths, with filesystem
calls moved into disposable copies of the executable so a blocked mount cannot
delay doctor's shutdown.

- `doctor/filesystem_paths.go` adds the check (`sandbox.filesystem_paths`, category
  `sandbox`) with Rust's constants (`PROBE_TIMEOUT` 2 s, `TOTAL_TIMEOUT` 8 s,
  `SLOW_PROBE` 1 s, `MAX_PATHS` 32), the summary/detail/issue strings, and the
  budgets line.
- `literalFilesystemProbeTargets` reads the materialized entries of the resolved
  restricted permission profile (`SandboxPermissionProfileResolution.ProfileJSON`),
  skips deny/glob/special entries and read-denied paths, merges access modes per
  path, and sorts by path.
- `ProbeFilesystemPathExitCode` backs the hidden `doctor --probe-filesystem-path`
  helper (`cli.DoctorOptions.ProbeFilesystemPath` + the dispatch in `app.go`),
  returning Rust's exit codes (0 resolved, 2 missing, 3 denied, 4 failed,
  5 not-probed-on-Windows) before any configuration is loaded;
  `runFilesystemProbe` launches the current executable with a bounded wait.
- Windows and read-restricted profiles list paths without probing; only missing
  and resolved paths are acceptable outcomes, so missing paths alone never warn.
- The app-server feedback doctor report budget moved from 25 s to 35 s, matching
  Rust, so the probes fit inside it.

Evidence: `doctor.TestProbeFilesystemPathExitCodeLikeRust` (resolved/missing
mapping, Windows skip), `doctor.TestLiteralFilesystemProbeTargetsLikeRust`
(literal-only, deny/glob exclusion, access modes, read-restriction flag),
`doctor.TestFilesystemPathsCheckListsWithoutProbingLikeRust` (profile id,
operation and budget details, access modes, provenance fallback, Windows listing
summary, and a clean status), `cli.TestParseDoctorProbeFilesystemPathFlag`, and
the updated `doctor.TestReportBuildsLocalChecks` check list. Verification:
`go test ./doctor/ ./cli/ ./app/ ./parity/ ./exec/ ./appserver/ -count=1` exit 0.

Recorded nuance: Rust's `source` detail attributes an entry to the config layer
that granted it (`permissions.<profile>.filesystem.<base>[.<subpath>] in
<layer>`) using the config layer stack's per-entry origins; `*config.Config` does
not retain those origins (they are built for `config/read` in the config
service), so Go always reports Rust's own fallback string, "effective filesystem
policy (entry provenance unavailable)". Rust uses that string for managed,
legacy, and runtime-added entries too, so only the config-attributed variant is
missing.

## sync46: the gateway-OAuth core lane, piece 1 - encrypted namespaced secrets

The plan's top core lane is #46318 (gateway OAuth credential management) plus
#46490 (compose it with primary provider auth). The first prerequisite is the
secrets layer: Rust's gateway credentials live in a dedicated age-encrypted
namespace with an independent keyring key, while Go's `safety` package wrote
plaintext JSON and had no gateway namespace.

- `safety` now mirrors `codex-rs/secrets/src/local.rs`: namespace files are
  `local.age` / `codex_auth.age` / `mcp_oauth.age` / the new `gateway_oauth.age`,
  age-encrypted with a scrypt recipient/identity derived from a passphrase held
  in the `codex` keyring service. Secrets never reach disk in plaintext.
- The keyring account is `secrets|<sha256(canonical home)[:16]>` with the
  independent `|gateway-oauth` key for the gateway namespace, and the canonical
  home matches Rust's `canonicalize` on Windows by using the `\\?\` verbatim
  form.
- Documents keep Rust's `{version, secrets}` shape, reject a version newer than
  1, normalize a missing version to 1, reject empty values, and are written
  through a per-process temp file plus rename.
- `NewLocalSecretBackendWithKeyring` makes the keyring injectable so tests never
  touch the OS credential store.

Evidence: `safety.TestLocalSecretBackendEncryptsLikeRust` (the stored file
carries the age header and a scrypt stanza and contains no plaintext, the
passphrase lives under the namespace account, and a reopened backend reads the
entry back), `safety.TestLocalSecretNamespacesAreIndependentLikeRust` (separate
files and keys, isolated maps), and
`safety.TestLocalSecretBackendRejectsInvalidDocumentsLikeRust` (empty values and
newer versions). Verification: `go test ./safety/ -count=1` and
`go test ./... -count=1` exit 0.

Dependency: `filippo.io/age` (pure Go) plus its transitive `filippo.io/hpke`,
with `golang.org/x/crypto` 0.53->0.55, `x/net` 0.56->0.57, `x/sys` 0.46->0.47,
`x/term`/`x/text` patch bumps and the unused `rogpeppe/go-internal` indirect
removed by `go mod tidy`.

Recorded nuances:
- Encryption compatibility with Rust is at the age-spec level (v1 header plus
  scrypt stanza); it is not yet verified against a file written by the Rust
  binary.
- Rust caches MCP-OAuth reads in-process (ciphertext and passphrase hashes);
  Go reads and decrypts each time, which is behavior-equivalent but slower.

Environment note: the C: drive has ~0.5 GB free, which broke linking with "No
space left on device". Builds and tests were run with `GOCACHE` on D: and
`TMP`/`TEMP` pointing at `D:\gotmp` (a dotted temp directory breaks the
pre-existing Cursor path-decoding tests, so the temp root has no dot).

## sync46 continuation: the shared OAuth protocol layer (#46318, piece 2)

Rust's gateway credential manager is built on a shared, caller-agnostic OAuth
protocol layer (`codex-rs/login/src/oauth/*`) plus a loopback listener
(`gateway_auth_callback.rs`). Go's existing `auth` OAuth code is
ChatGPT-login-specific, so this pass adds the generic layer the manager and the
#46490 composition need.

- `auth/oauthproto.go` mirrors `oauth/{pkce,authorization,client,error,diagnostics}.rs`:
  PKCE generation (64 random bytes, URL-safe without padding, S256 challenge),
  32-byte state, authorization-URL construction in Rust's parameter order
  (`response_type`, `client_id`, `redirect_uri`, `code_challenge`,
  `code_challenge_method`, `state`, then optional `scope`/`resource` and the
  caller's extras, appended to any existing query), callback parameters with
  state-first validation, form/JSON token grants through a client that never
  follows redirects, sanitized rejections (status, redacted request id from
  `x-request-id`/`x-openai-request-id`/`cf-ray`, the issuer's unmodified code for
  recovery plus the redacted display text, and oversized bounded bodies dropped
  whole), an opaque invalid-response error, and URL redaction for transport
  errors and caller-supplied issuer strings.
- `auth/gatewaycallback.go` mirrors the loopback listener: binds
  `127.0.0.1:<port>` (0 picks an ephemeral port), serves only `/callback`
  (404 elsewhere), rejects a mismatched state with 400 without completing the
  sign-in, maps a provider denial and a missing code to Rust's error texts,
  returns the success HTML, and stops serving on `wait` exit.

Evidence: `auth.TestGeneratePKCELikeRust`,
`auth.TestBuildAuthorizationURLLikeRust` (exact parameter order),
`auth.TestCallbackParametersValidateLikeRust` (state-first, provider denial,
missing code), `auth.TestOAuthClientExchangeAndRefreshLikeRust` (form grant
parameters and decoding), `auth.TestOAuthClientJSONEncodingAndInvalidResponseLikeRust`,
`auth.TestOAuthClientRejectionLikeRust` (status, code, redacted request id and
display text), `auth.TestOAuthTransportErrorRedactionLikeRust`, and
`auth.TestGatewayCallbackListenerLikeRust`. Verification:
`go test ./auth/ -count=1` exit 0.

Remaining pieces of this lane, in order: `gateway_auth_storage` (the
`provider-oauth|<sha256>` credential id, the `PROVIDER_OAUTH_<DIGEST>` secret
name, and the cross-process `secrets/gateway_oauth.lock`), `gateway_auth_token`
(`StoredToken`/`TokenResponse` validation and the bounded redacted endpoint
error), the `GatewayAuthManager` itself (cache plus pending-rotation persistence
under the lock, `WhenExpired`/`AfterRejection` reuse policy, browser authorize),
and finally #46490's composition with the primary provider authentication.

## sync46 continuation 2: gateway credential storage, tokens, and the manager

Completes the #46318 surface on top of the secrets layer and the OAuth protocol
layer.

- `auth/gateway_storage.go` mirrors `gateway_auth_storage.rs`: the
  `provider-oauth|<sha256(home, issuer, client, resource, scopes)>` credential id,
  the `PROVIDER_OAUTH_<DIGEST>` secret name (foreign account ids rejected), the
  dedicated encrypted gateway namespace, and the
  `secrets/gateway_oauth.lock` sidecar held from the first read through save with
  Rust's 50 ms/60 s retry budget (platform locks in `gateway_lock_{unix,windows}.go`).
- `auth/gateway_token.go` mirrors `gateway_auth_token.rs` and the config
  validation: token responses must carry an access token, a `bearer` type when
  present, and a non-zero lifetime; refresh tokens rotate only when the issuer
  returns one; usability keeps Rust's 30 s skew; the endpoint diagnostic is
  bounded, redacts the issuer detail, and omits it entirely when the token URL or
  resource carries a query; `validateGatewayAuthConfig` requires
  HTTPS-or-loopback URLs without embedded credentials/fragments, forbids OAuth
  request parameters on the authorization endpoint, and rejects an empty client
  id or a zero redirect port.
- `auth/gateway_manager.go` mirrors `gateway_auth.rs`: the cache plus
  pending-rotation state, the `WhenExpired`/`AfterRejection` reuse policy, the
  double-read under the cross-process lock, `invalid_grant`/
  `unauthorized_client`/`unsupported_grant_type` rejection handling that
  re-authorizes, pending persistence that survives a failed save, and the
  authorization-code flow through the loopback listener with an injectable
  browser opener.

Evidence: `auth.TestGatewayCredentialIdentityLikeRust` (id/secret-name shape and
rejections), `auth.TestGatewayStorageAndLockLikeRust` (encrypted namespace round
trip, exclusive lock, release), `auth.TestGatewayTokenResponseLikeRust`
(validation, rotation, skew, expiry), `auth.TestValidateGatewayAuthConfigLikeRust`,
`auth.TestGatewayAuthManagerRefreshAndAuthorizeLikeRust` (browser authorize,
cache reuse, explicit-rejection refresh and persistence), and
`auth.TestGatewayAuthManagerReauthorizesAfterRefreshRejectionLikeRust`.
Verification: `go test ./auth/ ./safety/ -count=1` exit 0.

Recorded nuances: the manager runs the refresh synchronously under its cache
mutex (Rust spawns a task so a dropped caller cannot abort persistence; Go's
equivalent guarantee is that the synchronous call completes unless the caller
cancels its context), and the HTTP client is redirect-free but does not yet carry
Rust's `ClientRouteClass::Auth` outbound-proxy policy, which belongs to the
#46490 composition step.

## sync47: compose gateway OAuth with primary provider authentication (#46490)

Closes the gateway lane: providers configured with `gateway_oauth` now carry
gateway credentials alongside primary authentication for inference, the
WebSocket handshake, and model discovery.

- `model/gateway_auth.go` mirrors `combined_auth.rs`: `composeGatewayAuth`
  resolves the gateway token, derives the delivery header (scheme + token) or the
  `Cookie` header (`name=token`, RFC 6265 cookie-octet validation), rejects an
  empty token, an invalid header name, an invalid token header value, a
  conflicting primary-auth header, and a missing runtime configuration, and
  replaces issuer errors with Rust's bounded message so echoed credentials never
  reach callers.
- `sharedGatewayAuthManager` mirrors `shared_state::gateway_auth`: managers are
  keyed by the OAuth configuration plus the codex home and held through
  `weak.Pointer`, so matching provider instances and model discovery observe the
  same refreshed tokens while dead entries are recreated on demand.
- `ConfiguredProvider` builds the manager eagerly and reports setup or validation
  failures when authentication is requested; `APIAuth()` composes the gateway
  header onto the primary credentials, and model discovery consumes that same
  composed auth (the remote models manager is built from `APIAuth`). The
  WebSocket handshake uses the same request headers, so it carries the gateway
  header too. `RuntimeProvider.GatewayAuthManager()` exposes the manager for
  explicit host login.
- `ModelsCatalogIdentity` digests the gateway OAuth configuration, so a catalog
  fetched through one gateway configuration is never reused for another.

Evidence: `model.TestComposeGatewayAuthHeaderAndCookieLikeRust` (header and
cookie delivery with the primary auth preserved and the input untouched),
`model.TestComposeGatewayAuthFailuresLikeRust` (empty token, sanitized issuer
error, missing runtime configuration, invalid header name, conflicting primary
header, invalid cookie value), `model.TestSharedGatewayAuthManagerLikeRust`
(matching configurations/hosts share one manager, different ones do not),
`model.TestModelsCatalogIdentityIncludesGatewayOAuthLikeRust`, and
`model.TestConfiguredProviderReportsGatewaySetupFailureLikeRust`. Verification:
`go test ./model/ ./app/ ./appserver/ ./exec/ -count=1` exit 0 and
`go test ./... -count=1` exit 0.

Recorded nuances: Go has no scoped provider-auth path, so Rust's
`api_auth_for_scope` composition is N/A; the gateway manager's HTTP client is
redirect-free but does not carry Rust's `ClientRouteClass::Auth` outbound-proxy
policy; `http.Header` has no sensitive-value flag; and Rust's
`AuthRuntimeConfig` reduces to the codex home in Go's registry key.

## sync48: theme-provided diff backgrounds (#46504, the diff half)

Rust's diff renderer resolves line backgrounds from the active theme's
`markup.inserted`/`markup.deleted` (falling back to `diff.inserted`/
`diff.deleted`) scope backgrounds, with an alpha-1 background acting as an
explicit terminal-default marker that disables the fill, and clears the gutter's
own fills when the line has none.

- `tui/diff_scope_background.go` adds the source: custom `.tmTheme` files win
  over the bundled assets, the raw scope background is preserved so the
  `#00000001` sentinel is recognised (the chroma mapping drops the alpha), and
  the resolved colour is quantized with `BestColorForLevel`.
- `tui.resolveDiffLineBackground` keeps the hardcoded palette as the baseline,
  applies a theme fill only at truecolor/256 colours (matching Rust's
  `RichDiffColorLevel` gate), and lets the terminal-default marker clear the
  fill.
- `diff_render.go` routes the line background and the gutter sign/number cell
  through the resolver, so a cleared fill also clears the gutter, as Rust does.

Evidence: `tui.TestResolveDiffLineBackgroundLikeRust` (palette baseline for a
theme without a diff background, the bundled dali/davinci themes clearing both
fills, a theme colour quantized at truecolor and 256 colours, the fallback below
rich levels, and a terminal-default marker clearing only its own scope) and
`tui.TestDiffGutterClearsFillLikeRust` (the gutter keeps a fill for the palette
and for a themed line and drops it for a cleared scope). Verification:
`go test ./tui/... ./app/ ./parity/ -count=1` exit 0.

Recorded nuance: Rust reads the scope backgrounds from syntect themes (the
two-face bundle supplies the 32 built-in themes); Go parses only `.tmTheme`
documents (user themes plus the six bundled assets), so a theme Go resolves
through chroma's built-in style names keeps the fallback palette instead of a
theme fill. Using chroma's generic `GenericInserted`/`GenericDeleted` background
would tint diffs that Rust leaves on the palette (catppuccin-mocha's port
background is a base colour, not a diff fill), so it is deliberately not used.


## sync49: upstream `a633ebc124..78245b47af` (25 commits)

This pass lands the core-surface changes first: the explicit turn triggers on
exec/TUI requests (#46569), explicit provider model catalog URLs (#46561), and
the shared plugin catalog discovery APIs (#46558), the macOS Seatbelt XPC deny
(#46583), the standalone network proxy binary (#46573/#46578), and the
agents-overview recent-session seed (#46579), plus the system-proxy bootstrap
fallback (#46562), the remote-model fetch metric (#46570), and the parity re-pin
to `78245b47af`; the TUI async-question notification (#46574) lands with the TUI
surface, which also drops the retired compact command grouping for individual
command cells and ordered in-group reasoning (#41893/#46487/#46565).

### Landed: explicit turn triggers for exec and TUI requests (#46569)

Rust sets `turn_trigger` explicitly instead of leaving it unset: `exec` for
`codex exec` turns, `user` for TUI (embedded app server) turns, and the goal
runtime attributes its automatic continuations with `goal`
(`ext/goal/src/runtime.rs`).

- `exec.Request` gained `TurnTrigger`; `execTurnTrigger` defaults a standalone
  run to `exec` and the Responses turn metadata now carries it.
- `app.runInteractiveTurn` (the embedded TUI turn) forwards
  `interactiveTurnTrigger`: `user` unless a caller requests another trigger; the
  local goal-continuation command requests `goal`.
- `app.remoteTurnStartParams` sets `TurnTrigger: "user"` on `turn/start`; the
  app-server goal continuation sets `TurnTrigger: "goal"` on its `turn/start`
  params.
- Go already preserved the active turn's trigger while steering (the steer path
  never rewrites `activeRuntimeTurn.Params.TurnTrigger`, and the agent
  controller propagates `turnTrigger`), matching Rust's updated steering test.

Evidence: `exec.TestRunAttributesTurnTriggerLikeRust` (exec default, embedded
TUI `user`, goal `goal`), `app.TestRemoteTurnStartParamsCarriesUserTurnTriggerLikeRust`
(wire `turnTrigger`), `app.TestInteractiveTurnTriggerLikeRust`, and the extended
`appserver.TestRuntimeRouterActiveGoalContinuationStartsTurnWhenIdle` (the
continuation's turn metadata carries `goal`).

### Landed: explicit provider model catalog URLs (#46561)

Providers may serve a Codex-native catalog independently of their inference
endpoint.

- `ProviderInfo` gained `model_catalog_url` (JSON + config parsing +
  `isZero`). `SupportsAPIKeyModels()` returns true for an explicit catalog URL or
  for the default OpenAI provider without a custom base URL;
  `HasProviderAPIKey()` reports `env_key`/`experimental_bearer_token`.
- `HTTPModelsEndpoint.CatalogURL` fetches the absolute catalog URL with the
  provider query parameters and `client_version`, bounds the response to 1 MiB,
  rejects redirects without forwarding credentials, and suppresses
  credential-bearing response diagnostics.
- Discovery gating now mirrors Rust's `uses_api_key_auth`: a custom base URL
  without a catalog URL keeps the bundled catalog even when
  `api_key_model_discovery` is on, for both the configured-provider and
  account-scoped managers. `ModelsCatalogIdentity` folds in the catalog URL.

Evidence: `model.TestModelsCatalogURLReusesAuthHeadersAndQueryParametersLikeRust`,
`...RejectsRelativeURLsLikeRust`, `...RejectsOversizedResponseLikeRust`,
`...RejectsRedirectsWithoutForwardingCredentialsLikeRust`,
`TestAPIKeyDiscoveryGateRequiresCatalogForCustomBaseURL`,
`TestProviderSupportsAPIKeyModelsLikeRust`, `TestProviderInfoParsesModelCatalogURL`,
`TestProviderHasProviderAPIKeyLikeRust`, `TestModelsCatalogIdentityTracksCatalogURL`.
The two managed-residency tests were re-pointed at an explicit catalog URL, as
Rust did.

### Landed: notify on asynchronous questions (#46574)

- `chatwidget` gained `NotificationAsyncQuestion` (`async-question` type,
  "Question: " display, priority 1, honored by the custom-notification
  allowlist).
- The async-question push site notifies only when the unanswered count actually
  grows, so duplicates and replayed history stay silent; the title is a single
  question's truncated title, `Question requested`, or `N questions requested`.

Evidence: `tui/tea.TestAsyncQuestionsNotifyLikeRust`,
`tui/tea.TestAsyncQuestionNotificationTitleFallbacksLikeRust`, and the
`chatwidget` notification display/allowlist assertions.

### Landed: deny XPC service lookups in macOS Seatbelt profiles (#46583)

`sandbox.buildSeatbeltPolicy` now emits
`(deny mach-lookup (xpc-service-name-prefix ""))` for restricted profiles.
Evidence: `sandbox.TestBuildSeatbeltPolicyDeniesXPCLookupsLikeRust`.

### Landed: shared plugin catalog discovery APIs (#46558)

Rust added source-neutral plugin discovery types and a batch listing operation on
`PluginProvider`.

- `plugin/catalog.go` adds `PluginCatalog`, `PluginCatalogEntry`,
  `PluginIdentity` (`remote`/`local`), and `PluginSourceLocation`
  (`cloud`/`executor`). `PluginCatalogEntry.String()` mirrors Rust's hand-written
  `Debug`: only the authored MCP server names are rendered, never declaration
  values.
- `PluginCatalogProvider.ListPlugins` plus `ListPluginCatalog` mirror Rust's
  `PluginProvider::list` default: a provider that only resolves roots yields an
  empty snapshot, and a batch provider's error propagates.

Evidence: `plugin.TestPluginCatalogEntryStringOmitsDeclarationValues`,
`TestPluginIdentityAsStringLikeRust`,
`TestListPluginCatalogDefaultsToEmptySnapshotLikeRust`,
`TestListPluginCatalogUsesBatchProviderLikeRust`,
`TestListPluginCatalogPropagatesProviderErrorLikeRust`.

### Landed: agents-overview recent-session seed (#46579)

Rust caps the command center's recent-session seed at `RECENT_SESSION_LIMIT`
(10) and shares the constant across pagination, collection, and truncation. Go's
remote dashboard source listed only `thread/loaded/list`, so recent but unloaded
sessions never appeared.

- `app.listRecentThreads` queries `thread/list` twice (interactive sources and
  exec/app-server sources), paged by `recency_at`, falling back to `updated_at`
  when an older server rejects the sort key, skipping ephemeral and child
  threads, then merging, sorting by recency, and truncating to 10.
- The recent metadata seeds the thread map so a later `thread/read` is skipped,
  matching Rust's list-metadata reuse; a failed seed only falls back to the
  loaded threads instead of failing the dashboard.

Evidence: `app.TestRemoteAgentsDashboardSeedsRecentSessionsLikeRust` (loaded +
recent merge, ephemeral/child filtering, the 10-session limit, recency order,
and the `recency_at`→`updated_at` fallback) and the updated
`TestRemoteAgentsDashboardReadsLastMessage`.

### Landed: standalone network proxy binary (#46573, #46578)

Rust added `codex-network-proxy --config <PATH>`, which runs the network policy
proxy from a standalone JSON file without a Codex permissions profile.

- `network.ParseStandaloneProxyConfig` decodes a single `network` object from at
  most 1 MiB, rejects unknown fields at every level (including nested MITM hook
  match/action/injected-header fields) and trailing JSON values, enables HTTPS
  MITM for limited mode or configured hooks, and requires
  `network.enabled = true`.
- `network.StartStandaloneProxy` binds the configured addresses directly
  (Rust's `managed_by_codex(false)` path) and `ProxyServer.Wait` reports the
  first listener failure, failing fast like Rust's `NetworkProxyHandle::wait`.
- `cmd/codex-network-proxy` wires `--config`, the process environment, and
  signal cancellation. Go validates Unix-socket allowlist paths for the host
  platform via `runtime.GOOS`, which is what #46578 fixed on the Rust side.

Evidence: `network.TestStandaloneProxyConfigEnablesMITMForLimitedModeAndHooks`,
`...AcceptsDynamicMatcherKeys`, `...RejectsUnknownFieldsAtEveryLevel`,
`...RejectsTrailingJSONValues`, `...RequiresEnabledAndBoundsSize`, and
`TestStandaloneProxyWaitFailsFastWhenListenerFails`; a smoke run of the built
binary starts and listens, and a disabled config exits non-zero with
`standalone network proxy requires network.enabled = true`.

### Landed: system-proxy fallback for login and startup requests (#46562)

Rust default-enabled `features.system_proxy_fallback`, kept it out of project
configuration, and retried eligible bootstrap requests through the system proxy
after normal routing failed.

- `features.Registry` gained `system_proxy_fallback`
  (`Stage::Stable`, default enabled), which matches Rust's registry entry
  exactly under `parity.TestFeaturesRegistryMatchesRust`; `Config.SystemProxyFallbackEnabled`
  reads the effective features (managed requirements included), and project
  configuration cannot set either system-proxy switch.
- `config.LoadCloudConfigBundle` bounds a primary attempt to five seconds and,
  when `FallbackHTTPClient` is set, retries through the system proxy after a
  connection failure or timeout. An HTTP status or decode error is final, and a
  caller-cancelled context stops the retry.
- `auth.ExchangeCodeForTokens` retries through the system proxy only on a
  connection failure before any redirect, using a ten-second bound and a fresh
  request body; HTTP errors, failures after a POST, and redirect-then-failure
  paths stay final, while a successful redirect chain is preserved.
- The cloud-config and login call sites supply the fallback client only when the
  primary already ignores the system proxy and the feature is enabled
  (`bootstrapFallbackHTTPClient`).

Evidence: `config.TestSystemProxyFallbackFeatureConfig`,
`TestProjectConfigCannotChangeSystemProxyFallbackLikeRust`,
`TestSystemProxyFallbackHonorsFeatureRequirements`,
`TestLoadCloudConfigBundleRetriesThroughSystemProxyLikeRust`, and
`auth.TestExchangeCodeForTokensFallsBackLikeRust`.

Go has no account-discovery `/accounts/check` request, so the Rust
`exec_bootstrap_get` account path has no separate Go call site to wire.

### Landed: Seatbelt scratch exclusions and rename guards (#46571)

Rust routes implicit process scratch access through the normal Seatbelt
exclusion rules and protects the ancestors of protected paths so a rename cannot
relocate them past their pathname carveouts. Go granted `/tmp`, `/var/tmp`,
`/private/tmp`, and `/private/var/tmp` unconditionally.

- The scratch write grants now carry an exclusion for every unreadable path,
  read-only subpath, and protected project-metadata path that lies under a
  scratch root (`(deny file-write* (subpath (param "SCRATCH_EXCLUDED_n")))`),
  emitted after the grant so Seatbelt's last-match-wins semantics deny it.
  Scratch reads stay governed by the profile's global deny-read policy, as Rust
  applies that policy to the exclusion set too.
- Every writable root's read-only subpaths now contribute their ancestor
  directories inside the root to `(deny file-write-unlink (require-all
  (vnode-type DIRECTORY) (literal (param "PROTECTED_ANCESTOR_n"))))`, emitted
  last so no broader allowance reopens the unlink used by rename — Rust's
  "renaming an allowed ancestor relocates its protected descendants" guard.
  A protected path that is a symlink contributes its resolved target's
  in-root ancestors as well (`seatbeltSymlinkResolver` /
  `seatbeltResolvedSymlinkPath`), and the subpath containment check handles both
  POSIX and native separators.

Evidence: `sandbox.TestBuildSeatbeltPolicyConstrainsScratchWithExclusionsLikeRust`
(the exclusion parameter carries the unreadable path and lands after the
scratch grant) and
`sandbox.TestBuildSeatbeltPolicyProtectsWritableRootAncestorsLikeRust` (the
ancestor unlink deny exists for a workspace-write root and follows every
allowance), plus
`sandbox.TestSeatbeltProtectedAncestorsFollowResolvedTargetsLikeRust` (a
resolved symlink target one level deeper contributes its own ancestors) and
`sandbox.TestBuildSeatbeltPolicyProtectsResolvedSymlinkAncestorsLikeRust` (the
same check against a real symlink; skipped where symlink creation is
unavailable).

Remaining in this lane: Rust's daemon-socket protection
(`codex_uds::shared_daemon_socket_directory` + `seatbelt_daemon.rs`) has no Go
counterpart because Go has no shared daemon socket directory.

### Landed: captured environment state for permissions (#46568)

Rust resolves fallback permissions from the thread defaults materialized with the
captured selection's workspace root URIs, including while an attachment is
starting or after it failed, and prefers the primary environment's own profile.
Go resolved one cwd-anchored profile for the whole turn.

- `config.ResolveSandboxPermissionProfileWithWorkspaceRoots` materializes a
  managed (and legacy workspace-write) profile with supplied workspace roots
  instead of the thread cwd — Go's counterpart to
  `materialize_project_roots_with_path_uris`.
- `turnEnvironmentWorkspaceRoots` returns the primary selection's declared
  workspace roots regardless of the attachment state (Rust
  `primary_workspace_root_uris`), falling back to the selection's cwd and then
  the supplied cwd.
- `turnSandboxPermissionProfile` now prefers the primary ready attachment's own
  resolved profile (Rust `TurnEnvironment::permission_profile`) and otherwise
  materializes the thread defaults with those captured roots
  (`threadDefaultSandboxPermissionProfile`). Because Guardian reviews
  (`guardianReviewPermissionProfileForTurn`), the permissions instructions
  (`permissionProfileForTurn`), and the skill sandbox contexts all funnel through
  it, all three Rust call sites are covered.
- `executorSkillSandboxContextsForTurn` keeps the thread resolution for the local
  and registered environments while each selected environment that supplies its
  own profile governs its own context (Rust
  `TurnEnvironment::permission_profile_with_workspace_roots`).

Evidence: `config.TestResolveSandboxPermissionProfileWithWorkspaceRootsAnchorsProjectRootsLikeRust`
(the environment-anchored profile carries the environment root and its relative
subpaths, and no longer the thread cwd),
`appserver.TestTurnSandboxPermissionProfileUsesCapturedEnvironmentStateLikeRust`
(a pending selection still anchors the fallback profile at its workspace root,
and a ready primary's own profile wins), and
`appserver.TestExecutorSkillSandboxContextUsesEnvironmentProfileLikeRust` (the
attachment profile governs its own context without leaking into the local one).

Remaining in this lane: Rust's daemon-recovery half (`daemon_recovery.rs` taking
the latest step inputs and refreshing readiness) has no Go implementation, and Go
still excludes pending/failed attachments from the sandbox-context loop by its
recorded #38684 decision.

### Landed: individual command cells and ordered reasoning in activity groups (#41893, #46487, #46565)

The TUI transcript still carried Rust's retired #38921 compact command grouping
(`MaxGroupedCommands`, `IsGroupableSource`, `compactGroupDisplayLines` rendering
"Ran N commands", and the tea Model's active compact group), so it diverged from
the upstream head in three ways. This pass removes the retired behavior and lands
the reasoning-order half of #46565.

- **#41893** — `ExecCell.WithAddedCall` now groups only adjacent exploring calls
  (`isExploringCall`, i.e. read/list/search parsed actions) and reports false
  otherwise; `ExecCell.ShouldFlush` is `!IsExploringCell() && all durations set`,
  so every non-exploring command renders its own cell. `MaxGroupedCommands`,
  `IsGroupableSource`, `allCallsCompleteGroupableSuccess`, `allCallsGroupable`,
  and the compact-group renderers are gone; the tea Model no longer tracks a
  compact group (nor flushes one at submit/slash/approval/elicitation/modal/turn
  boundaries), and each completed command keeps its own history message.
- **#46487 (partial)** — exploration stays open across a failed read (the
  `ShouldFlush` change above) and no longer splits on nonzero exits.
  Command *parsing* into read/list/search actions is still missing (see lanes),
  so exploring cells are not yet produced from live items.
- **#46565 (computer-activity half)** — a completed reasoning item no longer ends
  a computer-activity group (`isTranscriptOnlyReasoningItem`) and instead
  attaches to it through the new `tui.ActivityGroup`, which keeps ordered
  reasoning next to the calls that preceded it. Reasoning renders only in the
  expanded transcript: `codextui.Message` gained `TranscriptText` (the
  reasoning-interleaved variant) while `RawText` stays reasoning-free for the raw
  output view, and `ExecCell`/`ComputerActivityCell` gained
  `AppendReasoning` + `TranscriptLinesWithReasoning`.

Evidence: `exec_cell.TestExecCellNonExploringCommandsRenderIndividuallyLikeRust`,
`exec_cell.TestExecCellExploringGroupStaysOpenLikeRust`,
`tea.TestModelCompletedCommandsRenderIndividuallyLikeRust`,
`tea.TestModelCommandActivityKeepsFailuresVisible`,
`tea.TestModelCommandActivityRendersManualShellAndTurnBoundaryLikeRust` (all five
replacing the retired `Test*CompactGroup*` verifiers), and
`tea.TestComputerActivityKeepsReasoningInOrderLikeRust` (mirrors Rust's
`computer_activity_keeps_reasoning_in_order_live_and_replayed` snapshot: one
shared cell, chronological reasoning in the expanded transcript, and
reasoning-free compact/raw output).

Contract bookkeeping: `tui-compact-command-activity` was superseded by
`tui-command-activity-cells` in `parity/contracts/manifest.json`, and the
`parity.json` ledger item plus the `parity/domains.json` evidence note record the
supersession.

### Landed: remote-model fetch duration by authentication mode (#46570)

Rust tags `codex.remote_models.fetch_update.duration_ms` with `api_key`,
`chatgpt`, or `none` and starts the timer after resolving authentication.

- New leaf package `codex_go/metrics` holds the process-global recorder
  (`InstallGlobal`, `Global`, `StartTimer`). It exists because the model package
  cannot import `codex_go/telemetry` (telemetry reaches back through memories), so
  the recorder interface lives where library code can reach it; `StartTimer`
  returns a concrete nil-safe `*Timer`, mirroring Rust's
  `codex_otel::start_global_timer`.
- `telemetry.InstallGlobalMetrics` / `StartGlobalTimer` mirror Rust's
  `install_global_metrics` / `start_global_timer`, and `otelinit.BuildProvider`
  installs the built metrics client as the process-global recorder (Rust
  `OtelProvider::try_new`).
- `RemoteModelsManager` starts the timer on every catalog fetch with the
  `auth_mode` tag derived from `APIKeyAuth`/`HasAuth` (Rust's
  `has_provider_api_key() || is_api_key_auth`, then any-credential, then none).
  The TUI picker resolves its own credential, so it passes an explicit
  `MetricsAuthMode` that does not change the discovery gate.

Evidence: `metrics.TestGlobalRecorderInstallAndStartTimerLikeRust`,
`telemetry.TestStartGlobalTimerUsesInstalledMetricsClientLikeRust`,
`model.TestRemoteModelsFetchRecordsAuthModeMetricLikeRust` (api_key / chatgpt /
none), `model.TestRemoteModelsMetricsAuthModePrefersAPIKeyLikeRust`,
`model.TestRemoteModelsMetricsAuthModeOverride`, and
`app.TestPickerMetricsAuthModeLikeRust`.

### Parity re-pin to `78245b47`

The upstream checkout advanced to `78245b47af`, so the static pins were
re-pinned: `exec/src/lib.rs` (explicit turn triggers) and
`app-server/tests/suite/v2/mod.rs` (system-proxy suite) hashes, the
`app-server/tests/suite/v2` (149), `core/tests/suite` (215), and
`tui/src/chatwidget/tests` (95) fixture counts, and the `tui` snapshot totals
(1175 total; `tui/src/app/snapshots` 43, `tui/src/app/tests/snapshots` 62,
`tui/src/chatwidget/tests/snapshots` 53). The app-server v2 suite manifest
gained the `account_system_proxy` module (#46562), which
`appserver.TestRustAppServerV2SuiteManifestCoversRustModules` now requires.

### Dispositions for the rest of the range

- #46560 (stale environment config in a network-approval test) and #46559
  (rollout-budget accounting moved into `LocalAgentControl`) are Rust-side test
  and refactor changes with no behavior delta; Go already keeps the same
  accounting through `rolloutBudgetForSession`/`rolloutBudgetFollowUp`.
- #46557 (active-turn environment selection lookup) reads Rust's
  `initial_environments`; Go snapshots the registered turn's params, so
  `TestActiveTurnEnvironmentSnapshotRemainsStableAcrossLaterUpdatesLikeRust`
  already pins the same stability.

### Remaining lanes for the next pass

- #46580 Guardian reviews on the applied instruction snapshot.
  Go's Guardian reviewer is a separate model call that sends only the policy
  instructions (`guardianReviewPlanForTurn`) and never inherits the parent's
  user/thread instructions, so there is no applied snapshot to bind; porting
  needs Guardian instruction inheritance first.
- #46577 shared instruction-provider updates reaching subagents.
  Go has no `ThreadInstructionsProvider` extension API or root provider sharing
  (`AgentsMdManager`/`SessionInstructions` in Rust); its instruction sources are
  the `config/user_instructions.go` and `prompt/instructions.go` snapshots.
- #46575 Windows package identity for sandboxed descendants.
  Go's Windows launch paths do not carry the extended-startup-info attribute set
  Rust uses: the non-stdio `CreateProcessAsUserWithToken` path uses a plain
  `StartupInfo`, and the ConPTY path sets only `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_PSEUDOCONSOLE`
  (no job list, no `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_DESKTOP_APP_POLICY`). Matching Rust's
  head requires adopting the job-list plus desktop-app-policy attributes first
  (constants verified against windows-sys: policy `0x20012`, breakaway disable
  `2`, override `4`), and `current_process_has_package_identity` needs a
  `GetPackageFullName` kernel32 lookup. The packaged path cannot be verified on an
  unpackaged host, so this needs its own pass.
- #46571 Seatbelt scratch-directory exclusions (Go's simplified Seatbelt model
  grants scratch directly). The scratch write exclusions and the protected-path
  rename guards, including the symlink-resolved swap, landed (see above). Still
  missing: the daemon-socket protection, which needs a
  shared-daemon-socket directory Go does not have.
- #46572 turn-start cloud plugin discovery in the MCP extension.
  Depends on the cloud `PluginCatalog` listing provider Go does not yet have.
- #46567 separating plugin catalog listing from package resolution: the
  list/resolve split landed with #46558; the remainder is Rust's module move
  from `plugin` to `core-plugins`, which has no Go counterpart (single `plugin`
  package).
- #46564 plugin/app extension renames: Rust extension-registry names, contributor
  IDs, and tracing spans with no Go extension-registry surface.
- #46568 captured environment state for permissions and daemon recovery.
  The permission half landed (see above). The daemon-recovery half
  (`session/daemon_recovery.rs`) still has no Go implementation: Go has no
  daemon-recovery eligibility path that reads the latest step inputs or refreshes
  environment readiness.
- #46566 recovery commands while the current thread is unavailable.
  Go's TUI has no unavailable-thread restricted-input mode (no
  `pause_for_disconnect`/`handle_disconnected_key` equivalent).
- #46565 reasoning order in TUI activity groups (needs an `ActivityGroup`-style
  ordered call/reasoning store for computer-activity and exploration cells).
  **Closed** for both groups. The computer-activity half landed earlier; the
  exploration half landed in the continuation pass below (display command
  parser + shlex parity, appserver commandActions, `ExecCall.Parsed`, and live
  exploring-group accumulation with reasoning order).
- #46555 executor registrations across connection refresh and recovery.
  Go's exec-server `Client` recovers by session id through an `opener` closure and
  has no lazy/refresh client (`LazyRemoteExecServerClient`) or
  `ConnectionState.executor_registration_id`; the registration id it does carry
  (`remote.go`) is only a Noise prologue input for the relay/harness handshake.
- #46556 step settings and approval environments consistency.
  Go resolves deny-read paths locally; Rust's executor-context resolution
  (`get_unreadable_roots_with_context`) has no Go counterpart.

## Continuation: display command parser, shlex parity, and explored-cell actions

This pass closes prerequisite (a) and the parsed-action halves of (b)/(c) for
the #46565 exploration lane above (upstream stays at `78245b47af`).

### Landed: Rust `parse_command` display parser (prerequisite (a))

- `shell/display_command.go` ports `shell-command/src/parse_command.rs`:
  `ParseDisplayCommands` (`parse_command`), the shell-script path (`bash`/`zsh`
  `-c`/`-lc` unwrapping through mvdan.cc/sh), `summarize_main_tokens`
  (read/list/search classification for cat/bat/head/tail/sed/awk/nl/ls/eza/tree/
  du/rg/rga/git grep/ls-files/fd/find/grep/ag/ack/pt/python/Get-Content),
  connector splitting and simplification, and the small-formatting-command
  filters with their new third-party dependency (`mvdan.cc/sh/v3`).
- The full Rust unit-test module is ported to `shell/display_command_test.go`
  (every `#[test]` in `parse_command.rs`, including the PowerShell read /
  complex-PowerShell matrices, `is_small_formatting_command` cases,
  `cd`-relative reads, pipelines, and the Windows/backslash cases). Evidence:
  `go test ./shell/ -run 'ParseDisplayCommands|IsSmallFormattingCommand'`.
- Bugs found and fixed by the ported tests: double-quoted words are no longer
  rejected for glob characters (Rust accepts `"*.rs"` via
  `parse_double_quoted_string`), double-quote escapes now follow Rust's exact
  `\$ \` \" \\ \<newline>` rule, and single-quoted text is accepted verbatim.
- `singleUnknownForCommand` now mirrors Rust's `extract_shell_command` (bash OR
  PowerShell), so a PowerShell `-NoProfile -Command` invocation collapses to its
  script.

### Landed: byte-faithful shlex quoting

- `shell.shlexQuote`/`ShlexJoin` now implement shlex 1.3.0's `quoting_strategy`/
  `append_quoted_chunk` exactly (verified against a local cargo probe of
  `shlex::try_join`): `,` and `=` are quoted, backslashes use double quotes with
  `\\`, and mixed chunks are split. `shell.splitCommandLine` now mirrors
  POSIX/shlex backslash rules (literal backslashes inside double quotes, line
  continuations, and the trailing-backslash error).
- This closes a real divergence in the display `command` strings. The pinned
  `tui/chatwidget` expectation for a non-roundtrippable Windows command was
  corrected to the empirically verified Rust value
  (`"C:\\Program Files\\... -lc \"echo hi\""`).

### Landed: appserver commandActions from the parser (prerequisite (b))

- `appserver.commandExecutionActions` now projects `shell.ParseDisplayCommands`
  onto the client shape (`read`/`listFiles`/`search`/`unknown`) with read paths
  resolved against the executor cwd, matching Rust's
  `CommandExecutionPresentation::from_raw` / `command_actions_for_path_uri`
  (previously it only produced `read` from `shell.ReadPaths` or a single
  `unknown`). Evidence:
  `appserver.TestCommandExecutionActionsPreserveExecutorPathsLikeRust` (now also
  pins the Rust `shlex_join` command text for backslash paths) and the green
  `./appserver/` suite.

### Landed: TUI consumes parsed actions (prerequisite (c), partial)

- `tui/tea.parsedCommandsForExecCell` derives the display actions from the
  command's argv and `renderCommandExecutionItem` now populates
  `ExecCall.Parsed`, so `isExploringCell` is true for live read/list/search
