  configs either restricted or unlimited; pending/failed owner selections
  unavailable; unparseable selections unavailable). The MCP refresh fingerprint
  now renders the whole captured selection (not just the ids), so a config-state
  or policy change also refreshes the runtime.

### Evidence

- `mcp.TestEnvironmentPolicyExemptsOnlyHostOwnedAppsLikeRust` and
  `...PreservesSelectedPluginAndEmptyAllowlistLikeRust` mirror Rust's
  `catalog_tests.rs` cases verbatim; `...FiltersConfiguredServersLikeRust` and
  `...AuthorityOutcomesLikeRust` cover the configured/extension allowlist branch,
  unavailable selections, unselected environments, and the legacy fallback.
- `config.TestEnvironmentMCPPolicyParsesAndRoundTripsLikeRust` and
  `...AbsentAndEmptyLikeRust` cover parsing, matcher round-trips, and the
  nil-vs-empty distinction.
- `appserver.TestOwnerSuppliedEnvironmentMCPPolicyRestrictsServersLikeRust`
  drives the real router + MCP service: an owner allowlist admits only the listed
  server, pending and failed owner configs make both unavailable, a
  thread-supplied selection is unrestricted, and the policy is re-read from the
  stored selection on the next turn.

### Recorded nuance

Rust leaves a rejected registration in the catalog with
`McpServerDisabledReason::Requirements`, so `mcpServerStatus/list` reports it as
disabled. Go's `NewMCPService` omits every disabled registration (managed
requirements included) from `service.configs`, so a filtered server simply does
not appear. That projection difference predates this change and is unchanged by
it.

### Verification

- `go build ./...` green; `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file; `go vet` clean
  on `./config/`, `./mcp/`, and `./appserver/`.
- `go test ./config/ ./mcp/ ./appserver/ -count=1` green, and
  `go test ./... -count=1` -> 97 packages `ok` with no `FAIL`.

## sync75 addendum: remaining-lane re-audit

Re-checking the queued items against the current tree shows most of the sync39
triage table's "queued" rows are already landed (each verified by the named Go
symbol, not by memory):

| item | current state |
|---|---|
| `McpToolCall.mcp_app_ui` / `McpAppDisplayMode` (#45805) | landed (`appserver/mcp_app_ui_item_test.go`) |
| `ThreadStartParams.daybreakEnabled` (#45513) | landed (`appserver/daybreak_thread_start_test.go`) |
| `ThreadResumeResponse.collaborationMode` (#45519) | landed (`ThreadResumeResponse.CollaborationMode`) |
| `ConfigRequirements.allowedLoginMethods` (#45495) | landed (`config_requirements_login_test.go`, `runtime_router.go` projection) |
| `experimental_show_cell_overhead` (#46288) | landed (`config.Config` + `ShowCellOverhead`) |
| catalog descriptions for multi-agent V2 tools (#46297) | landed (sync72) |
| active plugin inventory in turn analytics (#46323) | landed (`telemetry.ActivePluginIDsAtTurnStart`) |
| MCP interaction / plugin install on the root thread (#46066/#45806) | landed (`MCPElicitationHandoffMessage`, `tool.NonRootAgent`) |
| declared onboarding skills in plugin details (#46544) | landed (`PluginDetail.OnboardingSkill`) |

Three unmentioned PRs need a prerequisite feature that Go does not model, so
they are recorded as lanes rather than landed:

- **#46324 broaden compaction fallback to the current model.** Rust's decision
  function (`core/src/compact_model_fallback.rs::should_retry_with_current_model`)
  sits inside the *previous-model* compaction path: a model switch captures a
  step context for the previous model plus a current-model fallback step context,
  runs pre-turn compaction on the previous model, and retries on the current one
  unless the error is `TurnAborted`, `Interrupted`, or `SessionBudgetExceeded`
  (`CompactionReason::CompHashChanged` / model-downshift only fire there). Go
  compacts with the thread's current model alone (`appserver/compact_remote.go`
  uses `record.Metadata.Model`); `compact.ReasonModelSwitch` is defined but never
  produced, so there is no fallback step context to consult. Porting #46324 needs
  the per-model captured step context first.
- **#46036 preserve config error causes when saving the approvals reviewer.** The
  fix is a TUI `format_config_error` call on the `UpdateApprovalsReviewer`
  persistence failure. Go's TUI has no approvals-reviewer save event at all (only
  `ApprovalsReviewerFromEffectiveConfig` on the read side), and no
  `format_config_error` equivalent, so there is nothing to attach the error chain
  to yet.
- **#46088 `--no-daemon` / #46117 `features.daemon_auto_start`.** Both build on
  Rust's `tui/src/daemon_startup.rs` eligibility model (shared-server probing,
  exclusions for Bedrock sign-in and `--profile`/`CODEX_EXEC_SERVER_URL`,
  `--no-daemon` propagation through resume/fork/archive). Go registers the
  `daemon_auto_start` feature key but has no daemon-eligibility or startup path
  and no `--no-daemon` flag, so the lane starts from that model.

`#46264` (relax the v2 `spawn_agent` delegation guidance) stays **N/A**: it
removes one line from Rust's long bundled description, and Go's bundled text is a
separate one-liner that never contained it (recorded in the sync74 section).

### Verification

- Each "landed" row was confirmed by grepping the current tree for its Go symbol
  and, where present, its test; the three lanes were confirmed by the absence of
  the corresponding Go surface plus the Rust prerequisite named above.

## sync76: separate Windows sandbox implementations from setup modes (#45737)

The sync39 triage table recorded this as "partially present: Go has
`config.WindowsSandboxImplementation`; the AppConfig projection is part of the
queued protocol sweep". The first half of that claim was wrong - Go had no such
type - and the PR was never landed. Landed here.

### The divergence

Rust #45737 split the legacy setup-mode type from the implementation type:

- `WindowsSandboxSetupMode { Elevated, Unelevated }` stays the setup RPC's
  choice.
- `WindowsSandboxImplementation { Elevated, Unelevated, Mxc }` is what
  `ConfigRequirements.allowed_windows_sandbox_implementations` carries, because
  the setup-mode type cannot represent the native (MXC) backend.
- The requirements source type is `WindowsSandboxImplementationToml`
  (`allowed_sandbox_implementations`, kebab-case, elevated/unelevated only), and
  the app-server projects it into the implementation enum.

Go used one `WindowsSandboxSetupMode` for both surfaces, which (a) made `mxc`
unrepresentable, (b) let `disabled`/`default` - setup-mode spellings Rust's enum
rejects - be stored in the requirements list and projected onto the wire, and
(c) accepted any unknown string in `[windows] allowed_sandbox_implementations`
instead of failing the parse.

### Landed

- `config.WindowsSandboxImplementation` with `elevated`/`unelevated`/`mxc` and
  `ParseWindowsSandboxImplementation`, which validates an entry instead of
  casting it.
- `ConfigRequirements.AllowedWindowsSandboxImplementations` now uses the
  implementation type for the struct, the app-server JSON projection, the clone
  helper, the cloud-config overlay, and both requirements parsers; the projection
  helper is `windowsSandboxImplementationsOrNil`.
- `[windows] allowed_sandbox_implementations` validates each entry (unknown values
  error, matching Rust's serde enum) while keeping the existing empty-list
  rejection; the legacy top-level alias keeps working and is validated the same
  way. `ResolveWindowsSandboxMode` and `ResolveAllowedWindowsSandboxSetupMode`
  compare implementations against the requested setup mode, so an allow-list can
  no longer contain the legacy `default` spelling.

### Evidence

- `config.TestWindowsSandboxImplementationParsingLikeRust` (the three
  implementations, case/space normalization, and rejection of `disabled`,
  `default`, `restricted-token`, empty, and unknown values).
- `config.TestParseRequirementsTOMLWindowsImplementationValidationLikeRust`
  (a multi-entry list parses into the implementation type; `default`/`disabled`/
  bogus entries are parse errors).
- `config.TestConfigRequirementsProjectsWindowsSandboxImplementationsLikeRust`
  (the app-server projection emits `["elevated","mxc"]`).
- The existing Windows requirements, sandbox-mode, startup-warning, and app/app
  server tests were retyped and still pass.

### Recorded remainder

`#46271` (enable `windows.sandbox = "mxc"` selection through configuration) is
the follow-on: Rust adds `Mxc` to the *mode* type as well and carries the
selected backend through environment configuration, command execution, patch
writes, sandbox tags, and TUI readiness, keeping `allowed_sandbox_implementations`
scoped to the legacy backends. Go's mode type is still `WindowsSandboxSetupMode`
with the legacy spellings, so the mode half needs its own pass.

### Verification

- `go build ./...` green; `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file; `go vet` clean
  on `./config/`, `./app/`, and `./appserver/`.
- `go test ./config/ ./app/ ./appserver/ -count=1` green, and
  `go test ./... -count=1` -> 97 packages `ok` with no `FAIL`.

### sync76 addendum: two more small-PR dispositions

From the same size-filtered sweep:

- **#45749 extract reusable Bash and Zsh startup scripts** is a **Rust-internal
  refactor with no Go delta**: it moves the interactive startup snippets from
  `shell_snapshot_capture.rs` into an exported `shell_startup_script`
  (`shell-command/src/startup.rs`) and calls it from the same two places. The
  scripts are byte-identical before and after, and no new caller is added, so
  there is no behaviour to port (`ShellType::Sh | PowerShell | Cmd` still return
  an empty script).
- **#46108 replace Sites migration state with a runtime compatibility guard** is
  a **newly identified lane, not a fix**. It removes Rust's persisted
  bundled-plugin exclusions, Sites migration checks, and migration wait, and
  replaces them with one loading rule: suppress `sites@openai-bundled` while the
  remote global catalog is active and a cached remote Sites install exists, while
  a missing remote bundle preserves the bundled fallback. Go has no Sites concept
  at all - no `sites@openai-bundled` handling, no migration state, no
  exclusion-based catalog filtering - so there is nothing to remove and the
  precedence rule has no Go landing site until bundled Marketplace loading and
  the remote-Sites cache are modelled.

## sync77: select the native MXC Windows sandbox backend (#46271)

The follow-on recorded in sync76's remainder. #45176's *identities* had already
landed (`sandbox.WindowsSandboxMxc`, the `windows_mxc` tag, the `WindowsMxc`
violation backend), but the *selection* half from #46271 had not.

### The divergence

Rust accepts `windows.sandbox = "mxc"` (`WindowsSandboxModeToml::Mxc`), keeps the
allowed-implementation list scoped to the legacy elevated/unelevated backends
(the constraint accepts `Mxc` unconditionally), and maps the effective mode to
`(SandboxType::WindowsMxc, WindowsSandboxLevel::Disabled)`. A selected MXC reports
Windows sandbox readiness as `ready` and counts as enabled in the TUI, so no
legacy setup prompt appears; when the native backend is unavailable the sandbox
transform fails with "native MXC is unavailable on this executor" rather than
degrading to the legacy sandbox.

Go's `parseWindowsSandboxModeValue` did not know `mxc`, so the mode was silently
ignored: level disabled, readiness `notConfigured`, and the TUI offered the legacy
setup prompt.

### Landed

- `config.WindowsSandboxMode` (`elevated`/`unelevated`/`mxc`) mirrors Rust's
  `WindowsSandboxModeToml` and is now the type returned by
  `WindowsSandboxModeFromValues`/`ResolveWindowsSandboxMode`, keeping it distinct
  from `WindowsSandboxSetupMode` (the setup RPC's elevated/unelevated choice).
  `mxc` parses, and `ResolveWindowsSandboxMode` returns a configured `mxc`
  unchanged even when the allow-list names only elevated or only unelevated.
- `appserver.windowsSandboxLevelForConfig` maps the mode to
  `sandbox.WindowsSandboxMxc`, so `windows_mxc` reaches sandbox tags/metadata and
  `windowsSandboxModeFromConfigValues` reports no legacy setup mode for MXC.
- `sandbox.DetermineWindowsReadinessFromState` reports `ready` for the MXC level
  (Rust skips the legacy setup and readiness APIs).
- `tui/chatwidget.WindowsSandboxLevelMxc`, and `app.interactiveWindowsSandboxLevel`
  maps a configured `mxc` to it, so MXC counts as enabled and never opens the
  legacy enable/elevated-setup prompt.
- `tool.runWindowsSandbox` and `startUnifiedExecWindowsSandboxCommand` fail MXC
  with "native MXC is unavailable on this executor" instead of silently running
  the legacy restricted-token sandbox.

### Evidence

- `config.TestResolveWindowsSandboxModeAppliesRequirementsLikeRust` gained the
  three MXC precedence cases (elevated-only list, unelevated-only list, no
  requirements), and `TestWindowsSandboxModeFromValuesLegacyShapes` pins
  `windows.sandbox = "mxc"`.
- `appserver.TestWindowsSandboxMxcSelectionLikeRust` (level = mxc, no legacy setup
  mode, legacy levels unchanged, and an elevated-only allow-list does not replace
  mxc).
- `sandbox.TestDetermineWindowsReadinessTreatsMxcAsReadyLikeRust`.
- `tui/chatwidget.TestWindowsSandboxMxcLevelIsEnabledLikeRust` and
  `app.TestInteractiveWindowsSandboxLevelSelectsMxcLikeRust`.
- `tool.TestRunWindowsSandboxRejectsMxcLikeRust` and
  `tool.TestUnifiedExecWindowsSandboxRejectsMxcLikeRust`.

### Recorded remainder (unchanged lane)

The native MXC launcher itself (`codex-rs/mxc-sandbox/src/native`, the
`--__codex-windows-mxc` arg0 dispatcher, Win32k policy building, and
`codex_mxc_sandbox::is_available`) remains outside the Go port, matching the
#45176 triage in `update/plan_2026_09_13.md`. Go now selects and reports the
backend faithfully and fails when it cannot be provided.

### Verification

- `go build ./...` green; `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file; `go vet ./...`
  clean.
- `go test ./... -count=1` green (two transient failures on the first run -
  `keyring.TestOSKeyringRoundTrip` from the OS keyring service and
  `appserver.TestOtelProviderReloadsAfterAccountChange` from its timing budget -
  both passed on re-run and on a full `./appserver/ ./keyring/` re-run).

## sync78: copy thread attachments into non-ephemeral forks (#45579)

The `attachment copy into non-ephemeral forks` item the sync39 triage table
queued. Go stores attachments in the thread record's `Extra` metadata (Rust uses
a dedicated state table), but the fork semantics are portable.

### The divergence

Rust #45579 copies the source thread's *current* attachments when creating a
non-ephemeral fork: the copies get new attachment ids and creation timestamps
while preserving resource identities and payloads, so membership can then diverge
on either thread; referenced resources are not copied. The copy is awaited before
the fork is published, a copy failure is logged and the fork still succeeds
without attachments, and resuming a fork does not copy again.

Go's `session.forkMetadata` clones the source metadata (including `Extra`), so
every fork inherited the source's `thread_attachments` list verbatim - the same
attachment ids on both threads - and *ephemeral* forks inherited it too.

### Landed

- `applyThreadForkAttachments(source, fork, ephemeral, now)` re-issues every
  inherited attachment with a fresh UUIDv7 id and creation timestamp while
  copying the type, identity key, and payload verbatim; an ephemeral fork gets an
  empty list; a UUID failure is logged and leaves the fork with no attachments.
  It runs before the fork record is saved, so the list is published atomically
  with the thread.
- Wired into all three Go fork paths so they agree: `Router.handleThreadFork`,
  `RuntimeRouter.handleActiveThreadForkRuntime`, and
  `RuntimeRouter.handleEphemeralThreadForkRuntime`.

### Evidence

- `appserver.TestThreadForkCopiesAttachmentsLikeRust`: a non-ephemeral fork's
  attachments match the source's types/keys/payloads with different ids; removing
  one on the fork leaves the source's list intact; an ephemeral fork's retained
  record has no attachments.
- The existing attachment lifecycle, fork, and state-runtime tests still pass,
  and `go test ./... -count=1` is green.

### Recorded nuance

Rust's copy is a single atomic store operation and its attachment storage is a
per-thread state table; Go's list lives in the fork record's metadata, so the
copy is applied in memory and persisted by the same record write that publishes
the fork. A `forkedFromId`-based client contract (fetch the fork's list rather
than expecting per-attachment updates) is unaffected: Go emits no attachment
notifications for a fork, matching Rust.

## sync79: `--no-daemon` and local daemon launch eligibility (#46088)

The first half of the managed-daemon lane the sync39 triage table queued ("Go
has `appserverdaemon` + `install`; the registry entry for `daemon_auto_start`
landed in section 1, the behaviour is the next step").

### The divergence

Rust's interactive TUI targets the shared local background server when the launch
is eligible and a compatible daemon answers, and keeps embedded mode when none
does (the target is `LocalDaemon { allow_embedded_fallback: true }`). `--no-daemon`
never probes or starts the shared server, even when one is running; the flag is
preserved through `resume`/`fork` and honoured by the session-archive commands;
`--remote`, `codex agents`, and `codex queue` reject it with specific messages;
and `tui/src/daemon_startup.rs` centralises the eligibility rules (exclusions for
`--oss`, workload identity, `CODEX_EXEC_SERVER_URL`, `--profile`, and
non-replayable configuration overrides, plus strict config and bypass-hook-trust).

Go had no `--no-daemon` flag and never reused a daemon: the interactive TUI only
used a server when `--remote` was passed, so a running shared server was ignored.

### Landed

- `cli.SharedOptions.NoDaemon` with `--no-daemon` parsed by the TUI-only flag
  parser (so the interactive root, `resume`, `fork`, and `agents` accept it in
  either position), the hidden `cli.AgentsOptions.NoDaemon` spelling merged into
  the agents launch, and `codex queue --no-daemon` parsing into the shared flags.
- `app/daemon_startup.go`: `daemonStartupExclusion` (`--no-daemon`, `--oss`,
  workload identity, `CODEX_EXEC_SERVER_URL`, `--profile`, then
  `daemonConfigExclusion`), `daemonConfigExclusion` with Rust's replayability
  predicate (only the unstable-features-warning switch and audited feature keys,
  boolean values or a non-empty boolean feature table, and no disabled
  shared-server feature), `daemonLaunchOverrides` folding `-c`, `--enable`,
  `--disable`, and `--search` into the override list the way Rust's
  `CliConfigOverrides` does, and `localDaemonEndpointForLaunch` (with an
  injectable probe) plus `defaultLocalDaemonSocketPath`.
- `runInteractive` rejects `--no-daemon` with an explicit remote (Rust's text) and
  targets a reachable local daemon when the launch is eligible, otherwise keeps
  the existing embedded path.
- `runSessionQueue` and `resolveSessionRemoteEndpoint` reject `--no-daemon`
  (without an explicit remote for queue, with one for the session commands) using
  Rust's messages, and `runAgentsCommandWithIO` rejects the agents combination.
  `interactiveStartAgentsDaemon` appends Rust's "The agents overview requires a
  shared server. Use codex --no-daemon to work without it." hint to a failed
  start.

### Evidence

- `cli.TestNoDaemonParsesLikeRust` (interactive, both `resume`/`fork` positions,
  the hidden agents spelling, and queue).
- `app.TestDaemonConfigExclusionOverridesLikeRust` ports Rust's
  `audited_overrides_allow_daemon_without_allowing_arbitrary_config` table
  verbatim (audited feature keys, the quoted-boolean rejection, the empty feature
  table, unknown and nested keys, and the disabled shared-server features);
  `TestDaemonConfigExclusionFeatureFlagsLikeRust` covers `--enable`/`--disable`/
  `--search`.
- `app.TestDaemonStartupExclusionLikeRust` covers every `exclusion` input,
  including the agents-overview exemption from the profile exclusion;
  `TestDaemonStartupExclusionEnvironmentLikeRust` covers executor selection and
  workload identity.
- `app.TestLocalDaemonEndpointForLaunchLikeRust` covers the eligible/reachable,
  empty-socket, unreachable, and every-ineligible case, and that the default
  probe finds no daemon when nothing listens.
- `app.TestNoDaemonRejectionsLikeRust` covers the interactive, agents, queue, and
  session-command combinations with Rust's texts.
- `go test ./... -count=1` green.

### Recorded remainders for this lane

- `#46117` `features.daemon_auto_start`: Rust can *start* the shared server for
  eligible new/resumed/forked sessions (opt-in, default off) and documents the
  `/daemon` menu and `/experimental` toggle. Go now reuses a running daemon but
  still never starts one implicitly; `daemon_auto_start` remains a registered
  feature with no consumer.
- Go's session commands (`resume`/`fork`/`archive`/`unarchive`/`delete`/`queue`)
  run a local runtime for their single RPC instead of discovering a daemon
  (Rust's `start_app_server_for_session_command` reuses the implicit daemon when
  eligible), so only the explicit-remote and `--no-daemon` validations apply
  there.
- Rust's configuration-*loader* overrides participate in `config_exclusion`; Go's
  interactive path has no loader-override input, so that input is absent rather
  than ignored.

## sync80: opt-in `daemon_auto_start` and the experimental-write opt-out (#46117)

The second half of the daemon lane: Rust can *start* the shared server for
eligible launches when the experimental `features.daemon_auto_start` flag is on
(default off), and requires a successful connection when it does.

### The divergence

Rust's `startup_orchestration.rs` computes
`auto_start_daemon = features.daemon_auto_start && !agents_overview && !no_daemon`
(plus not using a remote workspace). With it set and no exclusion it starts (or
attaches to) the shared server and, on failure, errors with
`daemon_startup::FAILURE_HINT` ("To work without the background server, rerun the
same command with --no-daemon ...") instead of falling back to an embedded
server. Go registered the feature key but had no consumer, so the toggle did
nothing.

The same commit also pins the experimental-features write rule
(`ExperimentalFeaturesView::write`): persist the value when it is being enabled,
when the server default is already enabled, or for `daemon_auto_start` (keeping
the daemon opt-out explicit); otherwise remove the key so the server default
applies. Go's edit rule was inverted on both branches - it removed the override
when disabling a default-*enabled* feature and persisted an explicit `false` when
disabling a default-*off* one - and its tests encoded that inversion.

### Landed

- `daemonAutoStartFeature` (the effective config's `daemon_auto_start`) and
  `daemonAutoStartStart` (start/attach through the daemon lifecycle runner and
  return the control socket) with `interactiveDaemonEndpoint`: ineligible launches
  never probe or start, an opted-in launch starts the server and treats a start
  failure as fatal with the `--no-daemon` guidance, and an unopted launch keeps
  sync79's reuse-with-embedded-fallback. `runInteractive` routes the endpoint
  through it.
- `experimentalFeatureEdit` now follows Rust's predicate (`enabled ||
  defaultEnabled || key == "daemon_auto_start"`), so disabling `daemon_auto_start`
  persists an explicit `false` while ordinary features restore their server
  default by removing the key.

### Evidence

- `app.TestInteractiveDaemonAutoStartLikeRust`: an opted-in launch targets the
  started server's socket, `--no-daemon` never starts it, a failed start is fatal
  with the guidance and no endpoint, and an unopted launch never starts the
  daemon.
- `tui/tea.TestExperimentalFeatureEditMatchesRust` gained the
  `daemon_auto_start` case and the corrected default-on/default-off expectations;
  `TestModelExperimentalWritesClearDefaultOverridesLikeRust` and
  `TestModelExperimentalPopupRetainsUnconfirmedOnFailureLikeRust` were corrected
  to Rust's mapping (pinned by Rust's
  `experimental_feature_writes_use_server_defaults_and_refresh_configured_values`).
- `go test ./... -count=1` green.

### Recorded remainders for this lane

- The TUI surfaces of #46117: the `/daemon` menu, the `/import` guidance
  recommending `codex --no-daemon`, carrying exclusion warnings through the
  resume/fork pickers, and the `daemon_selection_reason` / `daemon_auto_start`
  launch telemetry tags.
- Rust forwards the launch's audited server features into the start call
  (`start_with_features`); Go's daemon start takes no feature overrides.
- Rust excludes an unauthenticated launch that would show the Bedrock setup
  wizard (`daemon_exclusion = "Bedrock sign-in"`); Go has no Bedrock setup wizard
  on this path, so that exclusion has no trigger.

## sync81: the daemon auto-start launch warning and /import guidance (#46117)

The remaining user-visible surfaces of #46117.

### Landed

- `app.daemonAutoStartExclusionWarning` mirrors Rust
  `startup_orchestration.rs`: when the opt-in auto-start applies (feature
  enabled, no explicit `--remote`) but the launch is excluded, the TUI reports
  `Running without the shared background server: <reason> requires embedded
  mode.` It is never produced for `--no-daemon` (which disables auto-start
  itself, matching Rust's `auto_start_daemon` guard) and an eligible launch stays
  silent. `runInteractiveTUI` delivers it as a `historycell.StartupWarnings`
  cell through `Options.InitialHistoryCells`, the Go channel for Rust's
  `AppEvent::InsertHistoryCell(StartupWarningsCell)`.
- `interactiveExternalAgentImportDaemonUnavailable` now uses Rust's updated
  `/import` guidance: "Import from other apps is unavailable while Codex is
  connected to the local app-server daemon. Restart with `codex --no-daemon` and
  run /import." (Rust #46117 replaced the "Stop the daemon, restart Codex" text.)

### Evidence

- `app.TestDaemonAutoStartExclusionWarningLikeRust`: no warning without the
  opt-in or for an eligible launch, none for `--no-daemon` or an explicit
  `--remote`, and the exact `--oss` / `--profile` texts with the opt-in enabled.
- `app` and `tui/tea` suites green, and `go test ./... -count=1` green.

### Recorded remainders for this lane

- The client-side launch telemetry tags (`daemon_selection_reason`,
  `daemon_auto_start`, plus the daemon settings tags). Go records the
  process-start metric only on the *server* side
  (`telemetry.RecordProcessStartOnce(provider.Metrics(), originator)`, called from
  the app server and the exec runner); the TUI client has no metrics provider, so
  Rust's client-side tags have no Go landing site, and `RecordProcessStartOnce`
  carries only the originator tag.
- Rust forwards the launch's audited server features into the daemon start
  (`start_with_features(features)`); Go's `LifecycleRunner.start` launches the
  managed binary without feature overrides, so the daemon inherits its own
  configuration instead of the launching client's audited feature set.
- Rust excludes an unauthenticated launch that would show the Bedrock setup
  wizard (`daemon_exclusion = "Bedrock sign-in"`). Go has no Bedrock setup wizard
  on the interactive startup path, so that exclusion has no trigger (the
  `--bedrock-api-key` login command is a separate path).
- The `/daemon` menu itself is unchanged by #46117 in Rust (only its test file
  was touched), so there is nothing to port there.

## sync82: preserve tab indentation inside paste bursts (#45454)

The TUI lane's paste-burst fix.

### The divergence

Rust #45454 captures unmodified `Tab` events that belong to a non-bracketed paste
burst *before* shortcut dispatch, appends them to the burst, and refreshes the
burst window, so a pasted indentation is neither mistaken for a completion nor a
submit/queue shortcut; an expired burst is flushed first, so a manual `Tab`
keeps its normal shortcut, and the capture is independent of a slash context
(Rust's `paste_burst_tab_does_not_accept_a_completion` shows the tab landing in
the draft instead of accepting the completion).

Go's composer had no Tab handling in the burst path at all, so a Tab arriving
right after pasted text triggered the slash/skill popup completion or the
submit/queue shortcut.

### Landed

- `Model.noteComposerRunes` now records the last composer character time (Rust's
  `PasteBurst::note_plain_char`) in addition to opening the multi-character paste
  window, so the short-prefix case is covered.
- `Model.shouldPasteBurstTabInsertInsteadOfShortcut` mirrors Rust's
  `handle_paste_tab` decision: an unmodified Tab is captured while the burst
  window is live or within the burst character interval, and an expired burst
  lets the normal shortcut run.
- The capture is dispatched before the skill/slash popup handlers (mirroring
  Rust's ordering, where the paste-tab check precedes the popup dispatch) and
  before every composer shortcut; it appends one level of indentation through
  `insertComposerPasteTab` and refreshes the burst window.

### Evidence

- `tui/tea.TestModelTabInsidePasteBurstKeepsIndentationLikeRust`: for both a
  single ASCII prefix and a single Unicode prefix (`陌`), a Tab right after the
  character is captured (no submit), a Tab a half-window later is still captured
  and refreshes the burst, and a Tab after the window expires submits the draft
  with the pasted indentation intact.
- `tui/tea.TestModelTabInSlashContextInsidePasteBurstLikeRust` mirrors Rust's
  `paste_burst_tab_does_not_accept_a_completion`: with the slash popup open, a
  burst Tab becomes draft text instead of a completion.
- The pre-existing Tab shortcut tests
  (`TestModelTabSubmitsWhenIdleAndQueuesWhenRunning`,
  `TestModelQueuesPromptWhileRunningAndSubmitsAfterCompletion`, and
  `TestModelSlashCommandPopupFiltersCompletesAndDispatches`) advanced their
  clocks past the burst interval, because they previously pressed Tab in the same
  instant as the last character - a state Rust also captures.
- `go test ./... -count=1` green.

### Recorded nuance

Rust's textarea keeps the literal `\t` in the draft; Go's composer is
`bubbles/textarea`, whose public insert path sanitizes input text and stores a tab
as its four-space expansion. The pasted indentation and the shortcut suppression
match, but the stored character differs (this affects every pasted tab in Go, not
just bursts).

## sync83: manual compaction replaces the running turn (#46310)

The manual-compaction half of #46310, whose sync75 note recorded "stop the
running task before manual compaction adopts the saved environments" as still
needing a dedicated pass.

### The divergence

Rust spawns the compaction task only after
`sess.abort_all_tasks(TurnAbortReason::Replaced)`:

```rust
pub async fn compact(sess: &Arc<Session>, sub_id: String) {
    // Stop the old turn before the compact task picks up the next turn's environments.
    sess.abort_all_tasks(TurnAbortReason::Replaced).await;
    let turn_context = sess.new_turn_with_default_settings(sub_id, Default::default()).await;
    sess.spawn_task(turn_context, Vec::new(), CompactTask).await;
}
```

A manual compaction requested while a turn is running therefore supersedes that
turn: it stops, its terminal lifecycle reports an interrupted completion, and its
rollout `turn_aborted` marker carries the `replaced` cause. Go's
`handleThreadCompactStartRuntime` went straight to `TurnService.Start` without
touching the running turn, so the running turn kept its `ThreadManager` entry and
worker while the compact turn ran beside it, and no abort marker was recorded.

Rust's `thread_compact_start_inner` additionally enforces the direct-input policy
(`ensure_direct_input_allowed`) before it submits `Op::Compact`; Go's compact
path omitted it, so `thread/compact/start` could be aimed at a multi-agent V2
thread-spawn subagent.

### Landed

- `turnAbortReasonInterrupted` / `turnAbortReasonReplaced` name Rust's
  `TurnAbortReason` values, and `finishTurnInterruptedAnalytics` now takes the
  reason so it is persisted on the rollout's `turn_aborted` marker. The
  app-server still reports every cause as an `interrupted` turn completion,
  mirroring Rust's `handle_turn_interrupted`.
- `handleTurnInterrupt` is split into the RPC entry point and
  `interruptRuntimeTurn(threadID, turnID, requestedAtMS, reason)`, so a caller can
  supply Rust's abort cause without duplicating the review/hook/terminal-notify
  flow.
- `abortRuntimeTurnForManualCompaction` replaces the running turn with
  `turnAbortReasonReplaced` before `handleThreadCompactStartRuntime` starts the
  compact turn. It is best-effort, like Rust's infallible `abort_all_tasks`, and
  a no-op when the thread has no running turn.
- `handleThreadCompactStartRuntime` calls `ensureDirectInputAllowed`, matching
  Rust's `thread_compact_start_inner` ordering (load the thread, then the
  direct-input policy, then the retained provider route).

### Evidence

- `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterThreadCompactStartReplacesRunningTurnLikeRust`
  starts a blocking turn, requests `thread/compact/start`, and asserts the compact
  RPC succeeds, the running turn's completion is observed as `interrupted`, and
  every `turn_aborted` marker in the thread's rollout carries
  `"reason":"replaced"`. The test was verified to fail with the abort removed
  (`timed out waiting for turn turn-1 status interrupted`), so it pins the
  divergence rather than the implementation.
- `TestRuntimeRouterThreadListAndSearchReportSubagentDirectInputCapability` now
  includes `thread/compact/start` in the direct-input rejection table (the
  assertion requires the exact
  `direct app-server input is not allowed for multi-agent v2 sub-agents`
  message, which only that helper produces).
- `go vet ./appserver/` clean; `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file;
  `go test ./appserver/ -count=1` green (75 s); `go test ./... -count=1` green
  with no `FAIL`.

### Recorded nuances and newly identified lanes

- The abort does not await the worker's exit. `cancelActiveRuntimeTurnTracked`
  cancels the turn context and the terminal notifications are published exactly
  like the `turn/interrupt` RPC (in a goroutine, after the RPC acknowledges),
  because Go has no per-turn worker join handle - `TakeTurnWorkers`-style waiting
  is router-wide (`ThreadManager.WaitForTurnWorkers`). Rust awaits
  `abort_all_tasks` before building the compact turn, so it additionally
  guarantees the replaced turn has stopped mutating the thread before the new one
  starts; Go's cancellation is prompt but not joined.
- Direct-input coverage is still partial. Rust's `ensure_direct_input_allowed`
  (`b6333bb1bb` #40464, before the pinned range) is also called from
  `thread/revert` (`thread_processor.rs:2124`), `thread/shellCommand` (`:2447`),
  `thread/approveGuardianDeniedAction` (`:2482`), the MCP processor (`:546`) and
  the goal processor (`:312`). Go enforces it for turn start/steer, settings
  updates, the queue add/update/start methods and now compact start, but not for
  those five sites; landing them needs its own per-request mapping check because
  some Go handlers live outside `runtime_router.go`.
- #46310's other half - routing environment configuration and failure callbacks to
  the matching *active* or *future* selection so each can finish setup
  independently - still has no Go landing site. Go keys provisioning in
  `EnvironmentManager.records` by environment ID (`DeferredEnvironmentRegistration.
  CompleteReady` / `CompleteFailed`), not by the turn's captured selection, so
  there is no active-vs-future split to route; a differential test for a future
  selection finishing setup while the active turn waits is not present.

## Site check: #46552's completion routing, and the #40437 lane it exposes

The sync45 triage table left `7a15548c6a` "Move child completion routing into the
agent controller (#46552)" as *queued* with "Go's agent controller owns its own
completion routing, needs a site check". The check is done here; it does not
close as N/A, because the behaviour #46552 preserves is itself absent in Go.

Rust's moved `notify_parent_of_terminal_turn` preserves three observables:

1. best-effort, queue-only delivery of the completion message to the parent
   (`send_inter_agent_communication` with `AgentCommunicationKind::Result`);
2. a `SubAgentActivityItem` `{ id: "subagent-completed-{turn_id}", kind: Completed }`
   emitted to the *initiating* agent thread (resolved through
   `initiating_agent_path` when it differs from the parent's), and
3. result tracing recorded only after the parent accepted the delivery.

Go's `RuntimeRouter.deliverRuntimeAgentCompletion` (`appserver/turn_runtime.go`)
covers (1): it formats through `agent.FormatInterAgentCompletionMessage`, enqueues
into the parent's steer mailbox while the parent turn is active and otherwise
falls back to `enqueueRuntimeAgentMessage`. It has no counterpart for (2) or (3):

- `git grep "subagent-completed"` matches nothing in this repo, and the only
  `SubAgentActivityItem(` constructions are the *rendering* paths
  (`protocol.SubAgentActivityItem` from a tool execution's `subAgentActivity`
  payload in `app/remote_tui.go` and `exec/exec.go`).
- `agent/tools_v2.go` emits `subAgentActivity` only from the v2 tool output
  (spawn `started`, send/followup `interacted`, interrupt `interrupted`, wait), so
  a child that finishes *after* the parent turn end produces no parent-turn
  activity item, which is exactly the case #40437 added: "A Multi-Agent V2 child
  can finish after the parent turn that spawned it, so its successful completion
  needs to remain associated with that parent turn".
- `agentController`/`RoleAwareToolController` have no completion-activity entry
  point, and `notifyRuntimeAgentActivity` is the v1 collab mailbox
  ("Wait completed."), not a V2 activity item.

The underlying upstream change is `b705b6b076` "Report completed sub-agent
activity on parent turns (#40437)", which is *older* than the pinned baseline
(`5b1d656018`, 2026-09-14) and was therefore never in this session's triage
range. The missing half is specifically the controller-side `Completed` item: the
`Started`/`Interacted`/`Interrupted` activities are emitted by Rust's V2 tool
handlers (`multi_agents_v2/{spawn,message_tool,interrupt_agent}.rs`) and Go does
mirror those through its `subAgentActivity` tool output. Porting the completed
item means emitting it on the *spawning* parent turn - including a parent turn
that has already completed - persisting it as a canonical turn item in legacy and
paginated history, and covering the TUI rendering without counting it as a tool
call. That is its own pass; #46552 itself is a pure refactor of Rust internals and
has no independent Go delta.

## sync84: complete the direct-input policy across thread requests

Closes the lane sync83 recorded ("Direct-input coverage is still partial"). The
sync83 pass added the guard to `thread/compact/start`; this pass lands the five
remaining Rust sites.

### The divergence

Rust's `ensure_direct_input_allowed` (`b6333bb1bb` #40464) rejects app-server
input aimed at a parent-owned multi-agent V2 thread-spawn subagent. At the pinned
commit it is called from eight request paths:

| Rust request | Rust call site | Go before this pass |
|---|---|---|
| `turn/start` | `turn_processor.rs:390` | enforced (`handleTurnStart`) |
| `turn/steer` | same | enforced (`handleTurnSteer`) |
| `thread/settings/update` | `thread_processor.rs` | enforced (runtime settings update) |
| queue add / update / start | `thread_queue_processor.rs:292` | enforced (`handleThreadQueueRuntime`, `handleThreadQueueStartRuntime`) |
| `thread/compact/start` | `thread_processor.rs:2346` | landed in sync83 |
| `thread/revert` | `thread_processor.rs:2118` | **missing** |
| `thread/shellCommand` | `thread_processor.rs:2447` | **missing** |
| `thread/approveGuardianDeniedAction` | `thread_processor.rs:2482` | **missing** |
| `mcpServer/tool/call` | `mcp_processor.rs:546` | **missing** |
| `thread/goal/set` | `thread_goal_processor.rs:137` (`GoalAccess::Mutate`) | **missing** |
| `thread/goal/clear` | `thread_goal_processor.rs:279` (`GoalAccess::Mutate`) | **missing** |

`thread/goal/get` and `emit_thread_goal_snapshot` use `GoalAccess::Read` and
deliberately stay unguarded.

Rust's goal check has two shapes - a loaded thread goes through
`ensure_direct_input_allowed(thread)`, while an unloaded one reads the rollout's
session meta plus history for the latest `MultiAgentVersion`. Go's
`ensureDirectInputAllowed` already resolves both from the store record
(`runtimeRecordIsThreadSpawn` + `Metadata.MultiAgentVersion`), so one call covers
both branches.

### Landed

- `handleThreadRevertRuntime` rejects direct input right after the thread-load
  check, before the paginated-history check and the active-turn interrupt.
- `handleThreadShellCommand` now takes the `*Request` (`handleThreadShellCommand
  (request, params)`, called from the `thread/shellCommand` dispatch) and rejects
  after the thread-load check.
- `handleThreadApproveGuardianDeniedActionRuntime` rejects after parameter
  validation, before the event is decoded and submitted.
- `handleMCPServerToolCall` rejects after `ensureMCPThread` loads the thread,
  before the server call.
- `setThreadGoal`/`clearThreadGoal` now take the `*Request` (the `connectionID`
  is derived from it) and reject after the goals feature gate and parameter
  validation, before any goal state is read or written. Both honour the
  `GoalAccess::Mutate` shape above.

Each guard uses the shared helper, so the internal-request exemption that Go
needs for its in-process agent/controller RPC reuse is preserved.

### Evidence

- `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterThreadListAndSearchReportSubagentDirectInputCapability`
  gained six rows over the loaded `spawn-v2` thread - `revert`, `shell command`,
  `approve guardian denial`, `goal set`, `goal clear`, `mcp tool call` - each
  asserting the exact `direct app-server input is not allowed for multi-agent v2
  sub-agents` message. That string exists only in `ensureDirectInputAllowed`
  (`git grep` shows it at one production site), so a passing row proves the guard
  fired rather than some incidental error.
- The rows were verified to fail with the production edits reverted
  (`v2 direct revert response = ...`), confirming they exercise the new code.
- `go vet ./appserver/` clean; `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file;
  `go test ./appserver/ -count=1` green (75 s); `go test ./... -count=1` green
  with no `FAIL`.

## sync85: completed sub-agent activity on the spawning parent turn (#40437)

Closes the lane sync83 recorded when the #46552 site check found that Go had no
controller-side `Completed` sub-agent activity.

### The divergence

Rust `b705b6b076` (#40437) added `SubAgentActivityKind::Completed` and emits it
from the controller when a Multi-Agent V2 child succeeds, because "a Multi-Agent
V2 child can finish after the parent turn that spawned it, so its successful
completion needs to remain associated with that parent turn in notifications and
history". `7a15548c6a` (#46552) later moved that emission into
`agent/control/completion.rs` unchanged.

Go emitted `started`/`interacted`/`interrupted` activities from the V2 tool
output (`agent/tools_v2.go` -> `appSubAgentActivityFromExecution`) but had no
producer for `completed`: `git grep subagent-completed` matched nothing, and all
three TUI surfaces dropped the kind (the history cell returned `false`, the
status feed returned `""`, the buffered transcript produced no message).

### Landed

- `appserver.emitRuntimeSubAgentCompletedActivity` runs on the successful-turn
  path right after the child turn's record is completed and, when the child is a
  thread-spawn subagent with an `AgentPath` and a `ParentThreadID`, records
  `SubAgentActivityItem { id: "subagent-completed-{child_turn_id}", kind:
  completed, agent_thread_id: <child>, agent_path: <child path> }` on the
  *parent* thread, bound to the spawning `ParentTurnID` (kept in the item's
  `turnId` metadata), and appends it to the parent's rollout so the activity
  survives a reload of that already-finished turn.
- `appserver.notifyRuntimeTurnItem` publishes Rust's `ItemStarted` +
  `ItemCompleted` pair (`emit_sub_agent_activity`) for the parent thread/turn.
- TUI: `historycell.NewSubAgentActivity` renders `• Completed `<path>``,
  `AgentActivitySummary` maps the kind to `Completed <path>`, and the buffered
  transcript renders `Completed <path>` - matching Rust's
  `sub_agent_activity_summary` / `sub_agent_activity_title` / agent-status feed
  texts.

### Evidence

- `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterChildCompletionRecordsActivityOnParentTurnLikeRust`
  runs a child turn with `ParentTurnID` set and asserts the parent thread's
  record gained the item (id, `kind=completed`, `agentPath`, `agentThreadId`,
  `turnId`) plus the `item/started` + `item/completed` notifications for the
  parent thread and parent turn. Verified to fail with the emission disabled
  (`parent thread has no completed sub-agent activity`).
- `tui/history_cell.TestSubAgentActivityRendersCanonicalPath` gained the
  `completed` row, `tui/app.TestAgentActivitySummaryMatchesRustCases` gained the
  `subagent completed` row, and
  `tui/tea.TestBufferedTranscriptRendersCompletedSubAgentActivityLikeRust` pins
  the transcript text.
- `go vet ./appserver/ ./tui/...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file;
  `go test ./... -count=1` green with no `FAIL`.

### Recorded nuances

- Rust routes the activity to the *initiating* agent thread when
  `AgentTurnOutcome.initiating_agent_path` differs from the parent's path
  (`resolve_agent_reference`), and skips it when the child path has no parent
  segment. Go has no initiating-agent-path mechanism - its inter-agent
  communication does not carry one (`tasks/mod.rs` sets it from turn input
  items) - so the Go landing always targets the record's `ParentThreadID`. That
  remaining branch needs the initiating-agent-path plumbing.
- Rust's `sub_agent_activity_display` also feeds the agent-navigation "running"
  hint (`Started => true`, `Interacted => none`, `Interrupted | Completed =>
  false`). Go has `SubAgentActivityDisplay`/`RecordSubAgentActivity` but no live
  producer (only tests call it), so the navigation hint is a separate unported
  lane; it is not needed for the completion item itself.

## sync86: persist the TUI approvals reviewer and keep its save cause (#46036)

The last recorded item of the sync75 lane list.

### The divergence

Rust's `AppEvent::UpdateApprovalsReviewer` handler writes the choice through the
app server - `config_update::write_config_batch(..., replace_config_value(
"approvals_reviewer", json!(policy.to_string())))` - and on failure adds a chat
error message built with `format_config_error(&err)` (`format!("{err:#}")`, the
error chain), so the user keeps the config file location and parse error (#46036:
"Failures to save `approvals_reviewer` in the TUI hide the underlying
configuration error, leaving users without the details needed to fix it").

Go applied the reviewer only in memory: `applyPermissionSelection` set
`m.approvalsReviewer` (and the local state) and emitted the "Permissions updated
to ..." info cell, but never wrote config, and had no failure surface at all.

### Landed

- `settingsWriteKindApprovalsReviewer` joins the settings-write kinds, and
  `applyPermissionSelection` issues a `MergeReplace` edit for
  `approvals_reviewer` through `onWriteSettings` whenever the selected item
  carries a reviewer. That channel already reaches the app server's
  `config/batchWrite` for both the local (`interactiveSettingsWriteHandler`) and
  remote (`interactiveRemote...`) TUI, so no new wiring was needed.
- The write runs alongside `refreshStatusControlsCmd` through
  `bubbletea.Batch`, so the status surfaces still refresh.
- `applySettingsWriteResult` gained an approvals-reviewer branch: a failed save
  clears the notice and adds `Failed to save approvals reviewer: <err>` as an
  error history cell, preserving the backend's message (the config path and
  parse error) instead of collapsing it into the generic
  `Failed to save settings: ...`.

### Evidence

- `tui/tea.TestModelPermissionSelectionPersistsApprovalsReviewerLikeRust` selects
  an item whose reviewer is `auto_review` and asserts the single written edit is
  `approvals_reviewer = "auto_review"` and that the live reviewer changed. The
  `approvals_reviewer` key path appears in the production tree nowhere else, so a
  passing write proves the new path ran.
- `tui/tea.TestModelApprovalsReviewerSaveFailureKeepsConfigCauseLikeRust` fails
  the write with Rust's own regression text
  (`config/batchWrite failed: failed to load configuration:
  C:\codex\config.toml:1:24: unclosed array, expected `]` (code -32603)`) and
  asserts the message text is `Failed to save approvals reviewer: <cause>` - the
  `Failed to save approvals reviewer: ` prefix exists only in the new branch.
- `go vet ./tui/...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file;
  `go test ./tui/... -count=1` green (17 s); `go test ./... -count=1` green with
  no `FAIL` (97 packages).

### Recorded nuance

- Rust's `format_config_error` renders the anyhow chain, which includes the
  `config/batchWrite failed in TUI` context that `write_config_batch` adds. Go's
  settings writer returns the app server's error unchanged, so the Go message is
  `Failed to save approvals reviewer: <server error>` without that wrapper. The
  part #46036 actually asks for - retaining the config file path and parse error -
  is preserved either way.

## sync87: Windows package identity for sandboxed descendants (#46575 + #45550)

This pass started from a triage finding rather than a code lane.

### Triage finding: in-range commits hidden behind a theme label

The earlier sweeps disposed of the Windows sandbox service family
(#45455-#46241 in the plan's words) as "Rust-only structure". That is wrong for at
least one commit: `4199fda578` "Add opt-in registered package execution to the
Windows sandbox (#45550)" is *inside* the pinned range
(`git merge-base --is-ancestor 4199fda578 78245b47af` exits 0) and the plan never
mentions `#45550`.

Reproduction of the sweep that found it:

```powershell
cd D:\qax\reagent\dev\git\codex
git log --format='%H|%s' 5b1d656018..78245b47af   # 319 commits
# extract '#(\d+)' from each subject, diff against update/plan_2026_09_19.md
```

87 of the range's PR numbers are still absent from the plan. Many are genuinely
Rust-internal (Guardian/agent-control/analytics crate moves, SDK+Bazel CI), but
the Windows sandbox family is not: Go has no `CODEX_WINDOWS_REGISTERED_CORE`
capture, no execution-alias or staged-runner resolution, no service-managed
package registration or registration refresh, and no provisioning-service
preference for automatic setup (`#45550`, `#45542`, `#45559`, `#46239`), while
`helper_materialization.go` still materialises copied helpers - the pre-#45550
fallback that #45550 removes. Those are recorded below as a lane rather than
assumed away.

### The divergence (#46575, on top of #45550)

Rust 4199fda578 added `windows-sandbox-rs::app_package` with a bounded
package-name query and captured `CODEX_WINDOWS_REGISTERED_CORE` once at startup;
`fd59b42a09` (#46575) then replaced the "only a verified codex-command-runner.exe
may propagate package context" decision with the caller's OS-assigned package
identity:

```rust
pub(crate) fn current_process_has_package_identity() -> Result<bool> {
    let has_identity = current_package_full_name()?.is_some();
    ensure!(has_identity || !registered_core_requested(),
        "registered Core process has no package identity");
    Ok(has_identity)
}
```

and used it in both launch paths (`process.rs`,
`conpty/mod.rs`) to decide whether to add `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_DESKTOP_APP_POLICY`
(policy `BREAKAWAY_DISABLE_PROCESS_TREE | BREAKAWAY_OVERRIDE`), so a packaged
caller's descendants stay inside its package environment and can still launch
executables from the protected package directory.

Go had none of it: no package-name query, no registered-core capture, no
desktop-app-policy attribute, and both launch paths sized their attribute lists
for a single attribute.

### Landed

- `windowssandbox.QueryPackageName` ports Rust's `query_package_name` closure
  contract: `APPMODEL_ERROR_NO_PACKAGE` is *absence* (distinct from failure), the
  allocation is bounded by `maxLength` (32768 for a process package name), and a
  successful read must be terminated, free of embedded NULs, and valid UTF-16
  (unpaired surrogates are rejected like `String::from_utf16`).
  `RequestedValue`/`RegisteredCoreRequested` mirror `requested_value` /
  `registered_core_requested` (only the exact value `1`, captured once).
- `app_package_windows.go` adds `GetPackageFullName` via kernel32 and
  `CurrentProcessHasPackageIdentity`; the non-Windows file stubs it to "no
  identity".
- `ProcThreadAttributeList.PreserveDesktopAppContext` updates the list with
  `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_DESKTOP_APP_POLICY` (`0x00020012`) and the breakaway
  policy `2|4`, keeping the policy DWORD alive on the list;
  `ProcThreadAttributeCountForLaunch` mirrors Rust's `2 + u32::from(preserve)`.
- Both launch paths (`process_windows.go` `startupInfoWithExplicitStdio` and
  `conpty/conpty_windows.go`) now size the list from the identity decision and
  add the desktop-app-policy attribute when the caller is packaged.

### Evidence

- `windowssandbox.TestOnlyExactOptInRequestsRegisteredCore`,
  `TestPackageQueryDistinguishesAbsenceFromFailureAndBoundsAllocation`, and
  `TestPackageQueryValidatesReturnedLengthTerminationAndUTF16` are direct ports
  of Rust's `app_package_tests.rs` matrices (absence vs access-denied, an
  over-long request at both bounds, zero/oversized length, unterminated name,
  embedded NUL, unpaired surrogate, failed second read).
- `TestProcThreadAttributeListPreservesDesktopAppContext` performs the real
  `UpdateProcThreadAttribute` call against Windows and asserts the policy value.
  It was verified to be meaningful: changing the attribute id to `0x00020013`
  makes Windows reject the update (`The program issued a command but the command
  length is incorrect`), so the test pins the id/size against the OS.
- `TestCurrentProcessHasPackageIdentityReadsTheOsPackageState` exercises the real
  `GetPackageFullName` call and asserts consistency with the decision function
  (skipping only when the host itself requests registered-core execution without
  an identity).
- `go vet ./sandbox/...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file;
  `go test ./sandbox/... -count=1` green; `go test ./... -count=1` green with no
  `FAIL` (97 packages).

### Recorded lane: Windows service-managed registered package execution (#45550 and siblings)

Not yet landed, with the evidence gathered here:

- `#45550` capture + propagation of `CODEX_WINDOWS_REGISTERED_CORE` to sandbox
  wrappers (the capture landed here; the wrapper-setup-env propagation has no Go
  counterpart - Go has no wrapper setup env allowlist function), execution-alias
  launch of registered runners, staged-runner verification, "require service
  provisioning without falling back to copied helpers or `PATH` lookup"
  (`helper_materialization.go` is that fallback), readiness refresh of stale
  package registrations, and proxy reconciliation through the service.
- `#45542` service-managed package registration for sandbox accounts, `#45559`
  resuming registration refresh after service restarts, `#46239` preferring the
  provisioning service for automatic setup, `#46237`/`#46043`/`#46038`/`#45799`
  error details, password repair, DACL tests and uninstall cleanup.
- `#45813`/`#45821`/`#45830`/`#45757` wire Windows sandbox policy/state into the
  TUI and managed proxy routing.

## sync88: imagegen description guidance (#45544)

Continuing the untriaged-commit sweep from sync87.

### The divergence

`2f1583b411` (#45544) added one guideline line to
`codex-rs/ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md`, which Rust embeds with
`include_str!` and passes verbatim as the model-visible description
(`ext/image-generation/src/tool.rs:635` `description: IMAGEGEN_DESCRIPTION`):

```
- Avoid printing the full result or its base64 image data with `text()` or `notify()`; print only small metadata when needed.
```

Go vendors the same text in `turn/image_generation.go`
(`imageGenerationDescription`, used by `(*ImageGenerationHandler).Spec()`), and was
missing that line.

### Landed

- Added the guideline to `turn/image_generation.go` at Rust's position (directly
  after the "imagegen needs a few minutes" line) and to the golden in
  `turn/image_generation_test.go`.
- Added `parity.TestRustImagegenDescriptionMatchesGo`, a live blob check: it reads
  `codex-rs/ext/image-generation/imagegen_description.md` from the checkout's
  object store (`git show HEAD:<path>`, so the CRLF of a Windows working tree
  cannot mask a difference) and compares it with
  `turn.NewImageGenerationHandler(nil).Spec().Description`.

### Evidence

- The parity test was verified to be meaningful by corrupting the Go text
  ("...small metadata when needed." -> "...small metadata."), which fails it.
  A file-based check without the object store was tried first and failed on the
  working tree's CRLF endings, which is why the test reads the blob.
- `go test ./turn/ ./parity/ -count=1` green; `go test ./... -count=1` green with
  no `FAIL`.

### Sweep record: untriaged commits worth auditing next

87 of the range's PR numbers are still absent from this plan (method in sync87).
Filtering those commits by diff shape surfaces the following Go-visible
candidates; each needs a per-commit Go-side check because the earlier
theme-level dispositions are unreliable:

- Telemetry/metrics: `3fa9039bd7` #45461 adds `stage`/`error_kind`/`trigger` tags
  to the rollout-compression failure counters. Go's `rollout/compression.go` emits
  no `codex.rollout_compression.*` metric at all, so the whole metrics module
  (`metrics.rs` counters, histograms, durations, ratio, scan and temp-cleanup) is

- Core prompts/instructions: #46075 (captured step settings when spawning
  subagents), #45711 (startup tool allowlists for threads), #46026 (centralized
  model-message rendering).
- TUI/app-server permission routing: #46008 (built-in permission selections
  through the app server), #46013 (permission shortcuts through the shared

  Go's `applyPermissionSelection` still applies built-in presets locally and its
  unsupported-server text is still "Named profiles require a newer app server.".
- History/session: #45980 (summary-history fallback for read-only conversations),
  #45549 (flush answer/plan streams on turn termination).
- Exec-server/sandbox: #46031 (Noise relay streams after handshake failures),
  #46125 (daemon socket isolation checks for private tmp mounts), #45837/#45811
  (WSLg root, bounded WSL detection).
- N/A by construction: #45760 (V8 array-sort paths; Go's code mode has no V8),

  (Rust test-only or snapshot-only), #45420 (Guardian sampler module move).

## sync89: Noise relay handshake cooldown (#46031)

### The divergence

Rust `105fe8761c` (#46031): exhausting the Noise handshake failure budget used to
close the physical relay, "disconnecting authenticated streams along with failed
attempts". The fix pauses *new handshake admission* for
`NOISE_HANDSHAKE_FAILURE_COOLDOWN` (10s) instead:

```rust
fn record_failed_handshake(failed: &mut usize, cooldown_until: &mut Option<Instant>) {
    if cooldown_until.is_some() { return; }        // failures during the cooldown do not extend it
    *failed += 1;
    if *failed >= MAX_FAILED_NOISE_HANDSHAKES {
        *cooldown_until = Some(Instant::now() + NOISE_HANDSHAKE_FAILURE_COOLDOWN);
    }
}
```

and an incoming `Handshake` frame is rejected before it is parsed while the
cooldown is active, with `failed_handshakes = 0` (and the cooldown cleared) as
soon as admission resumes.

Go's `execserver/remote_relay.go` mirrored the pre-fix behaviour at five sites,
each doing `failedHandshakes++; if >= 8 { return errors.New("closing Noise relay
after repeated handshake failures") }`.

### Landed

- `recordFailedHandshake(&failedHandshakes, &handshakeCooldownUntil)` ports Rust's
  helper, and all five failure sites (registry rejection, handshake completion
  failure, duplicate pending handshake, malformed/oversized handshake, early data
  for a pending stream) now record through it instead of returning an error.
- The `relayFrameHandshake` arm rejects with a reset while the cooldown is active,
  before the duplicate/pending checks and before parsing the hybrid handshake; on
  the first handshake after expiry it resets `failedHandshakes` and clears the
  cooldown.
- `noiseHandshakeFailureCooldown` is a package variable (default 10s) so tests can
  shorten the window instead of waiting it out; Rust's constant is a const and its
  tests drive tokio's clock.

### Evidence

- `execserver.TestRepeatedHandshakeFailuresPauseAdmissionWithoutDisconnectingLikeRust`:
  eight registry rejections exhaust the budget (asserting exactly eight
  validations), a handshake during the cooldown is reset without a registry
  validation, and after the cooldown a new handshake is admitted again (ninth
  validation) with the budget reset - while the relay never terminates.
- `execserver.TestMalformedHandshakesPreservePhysicalRelayLikeRust` and
  `TestRepeatedEarlyDataDuringValidationPausesAdmissionLikeRust` cover the
  malformed-signature and early-data failure paths.
- Verified to be meaningful: restoring the old `return errors.New("closing Noise
  relay ...")` in the registry-rejection path makes the first test fail
  (`read relay reset: ... EOF`, i.e. the relay had closed).
- Tests were re-run three times and the full package passes; `go vet ./execserver/`
  clean; `go test ./execserver/ -count=1` and `go test ./... -count=1` green.

### Recorded nuance

Rust's `malformed_handshakes_preserve_active_streams_and_admission_recovers` also
carries encrypted traffic over an *established* stream during the cooldown (its
`EchoMessages` harness). Go's relay test rig terminates Noise streams into the
exec-server's JSON-RPC handler rather than an echo handler, so that specific
assertion is not ported: the guarantee now follows from not closing the relay,
and the pause/resume behaviour is covered by the tests above.

### sync89 addendum: dispositions for three more untriaged commits

- `105fe8761c` #46031 (Noise relay handshake cooldown): **landed in sync89**.
- `4701aa4b42` #45933 (avoid redundant model catalog lookups in reused Guardian
  reviewers): the change is an early return inside Rust's
  `maybe_run_previous_model_inline_compact` - the same not-yet-ported
  previous-model compaction path that #46324 needs. Recorded with that lane
  rather than landed separately.
- `8f9d0e4652` #45811 (bound WSL terminal detection; disable keyboard
  enhancements for VS Code/unknown on WSL): Go's `tui/terminal_probe.go` ports the
  keyboard-enhancement probe, but `StartupKeyboardEnhancementProbeSkip` has no
  producer and there is no `CODEX_TUI_DISABLE_KEYBOARD_ENHANCEMENT` handling, so
  Rust's `keyboard_enhancement_disabled` decision (env override, WSL detection,
  the three-way VS Code/other/unknown result with a bounded Windows probe) has no
  Go counterpart. It is a real lane: on WSL with VS Code, inconclusive detection
  must leave enhancements disabled or dead-key composition breaks.

## sync90: ConPTY output closes after the last console client exits (#45504)

### The divergence

Rust `6ce16aadce` (#45504): "Retaining the pseudoconsole's creation pipe handles
prevents output readers from seeing EOF while the session remains alive." The fix
drops `PsuedoCon`'s creation handles right after a successful `CreateProcessW`
and calls `ReleasePseudoConsole` when kernel32 exports it (Windows 11 24H2+), so
the console closes its output once the last attached client exits while surviving
console descendants keep their I/O. Older Windows keeps the
`ClosePseudoConsole`-on-drop path.

Go's `sandbox/windowssandbox/conpty` kept all four pipe handles in `Instance`
until `Close`, at both spawn sites: `conpty.SpawnProcessAsUserWithToken` and
`execserver.startExecServerTTY` (which builds its own attribute list and
`CreateProcess` call). Readers therefore blocked for as long as the session
lived.

### Landed

- `conpty.Instance.FinishSpawn()` drops the creation handles (closing and zeroing
  `inputRead`/`outputWrite` while leaving the client ends untouched) and then
  calls `ReleasePseudoConsole` when available, warning on a non-`S_OK` HRESULT.
  Both spawn sites call it after their `CreateProcess*` succeeds.
- `conpty.ReleasePseudoConsoleAvailable()` exposes the lazy kernel32 probe
  (false off Windows).

### Evidence

- `sandbox/windowssandbox/conpty.TestInstanceDropCreationHandlesClosesCreationPipes`:
  a real pseudoconsole's creation handles are closed and zeroed, the client ends
  survive, a second drop is a no-op, and `Close` still succeeds.
- `sandbox/windowssandbox/conpty.TestReleasePseudoConsoleOwnershipSucceedsWhenAvailable`
  calls `ReleasePseudoConsole` on a real `HPCON` through the same lazy proc the
  production code uses and asserts `S_OK`. It ran (not skipped) on this host.
- `sandbox/windowssandbox/conpty.TestSpawnedConsoleOutputReachesEOFAfterLastClientExits`
  performs a real restricted-token ConPTY spawn and asserts the creation handles
  are dropped and that a `PeekNamedPipe` probe on the output pipe reports EOF
  within 10s (observed at ~50ms).
- `execserver.TestExecServerTTYOutputReachesEOFWithoutClosingThePTY` starts a real
  exec-server TTY session and asserts the reader reaches EOF after the child
  exits *without* the caller closing the PTY. Verified to fail (10.3s block) when
  `FinishSpawn()` is removed from that path, and the conpty test fails when the
  call is removed from `SpawnProcessAsUserWithToken`.
- Measured before/after on this host: with the creation handles retained the
  output reader blocks beyond 12s (Rust's pre-fix behaviour); after the change it
  observes `ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE` promptly.
- `go vet ./sandbox/... ./execserver/` clean; `gofmt -l` clean; `go test
  ./execserver/ ./sandbox/windowssandbox/conpty/ -count=1` green; `go test ./...
  -count=1` green (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Recorded observations

- On this host `cmd /c echo` console output never reaches the ConPTY output pipe
  in either variant (with or without the fix), which is why the pre-existing
  exec-server probe helper skips when the probe string is missing. The property
  pinned here is therefore the EOF/reader-lifetime behaviour, which is what
  #45504 changes.
- `execserver/tty_test_windows.go` and `execserver/tty_test_unix.go` do not end in
  `_test.go`, so Go compiles them into the production package (their helper
  imports `testing`). Left as-is rather than renamed here; noted because it is
  why the exec-server ConPTY test had to live in its own `_test.go` file.

## sync91: spawns inherit the invoking step's captured settings (#46075)

### The divergence

Rust `800d183e2d` (#46075): "After a settings update during an active turn,
spawned agents could inherit the turn's initial model and reasoning settings."

`prepare_agent_spawn_config` now takes the invoking `StepContext` and builds the
child from `step_context.settings`:

```rust
config.model = Some(settings.model_info.slug.clone());
config.model_reasoning_effort = settings.effective_reasoning_effort();
```

before `apply_requested_spawn_agent_model_overrides` layers the requested
model/effort on top.

Go's `runtimeAgentController.SpawnAgent` resolved the child model as
`args.Model` else the **parent thread record's** `Metadata.Model`, and passed an
effort only from `args.ReasoningEffort` and a summary never - so a mid-turn
settings update (which Go applies through
`updateActiveTurnRuntimeSettings` on the live turn params) did not reach
subagents, and every child silently lost the parent's effort/summary.

### Landed

- `runtimeAgentController.capturedSpawnSettings()` reads the invoking turn's live
  params (the Go analog of Rust's per-step captured settings) and returns the
  captured model, the *effective* reasoning effort (`appReasoningEffortForTurn`,
  i.e. params over config including the plan-mode case) and the captured
  reasoning summary. It only contributes when the controller's own turn is the
  active one, so a stale controller cannot inherit a newer turn's settings.
- `SpawnAgent` uses that model beneath an explicit `args.Model`, and now passes
  the captured effort (beneath an explicit `args.ReasoningEffort`) and the
  captured summary to the child turn - for both multi-agent versions.

### Evidence

- `appserver.TestRuntimeAgentControllerSpawnUsesCapturedStepSettingsLikeRust`
  registers an invoking turn whose live params carry `model=gpt-5.4`,
  `effort=high`, `summary=concise` while the parent record still says
  `gpt-5.4-old`, then spawns and asserts the child record's model and the child
  turn's effort/summary. It fails before the change
  (`child model = "gpt-5.4-old"`) and also pins that an explicit spawn model and
  effort override the captured values.
- `go vet ./appserver/` clean; `gofmt -l` clean; `go test ./appserver/ -count=1`
  green (126 s); `go test ./... -count=1` green (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Recorded remainder for this lane

Rust's `apply_requested_spawn_agent_model_overrides` also (a) resolves a
requested model - or the config's `agent_default_subagent_model` - through the
model catalog with per-version aliasing (`find_spawn_agent_model_name`),
(b) validates a requested effort against that model's
`supported_reasoning_levels` (`validate_spawn_agent_reasoning_effort`), and
(c) falls back to the model's `default_reasoning_level` when only a model is
requested. Go has no spawn-model catalog validation at all: `find_spawn_agent_model_name`
and `validate_spawn_agent_reasoning_effort` have no counterparts, and
`agent_default_subagent_model` appears only in usage-tag tables, so that half of
the lane needs its own pass.

## sync92: spawn model/effort overrides are resolved and validated (#46075 remainder)

The remainder sync91 recorded: Rust's `apply_requested_spawn_agent_model_overrides`
also resolves the requested model through the catalog, validates the requested
effort against that model, and falls back to the model's default reasoning level.
Go's `exec` lane had a private copy of that logic; the appserver lane
(`runtimeAgentController`) had none, and neither lane applied the configured
subagent defaults.

### The divergence

Rust (`core/src/agent/child_config.rs`):

- `requested_model = requested_model.or(config.agent_default_subagent_model)` and
  `requested_reasoning_effort = requested_reasoning_effort.or(config.agent_default_subagent_reasoning_effort)`;
  with neither present the captured settings stand untouched.
- a requested model must satisfy `find_spawn_agent_model_name`, whose filter is
  `show_in_picker` (= `visibility == List`) plus
  `model_supports_multi_agent_backend` (`version != V2 || preset.multi_agent_version != Disabled`),
  and whose error lists the picker-visible candidates capped at
  `MAX_SPAWN_AGENT_MODEL_OVERRIDES` (5):
  `Unknown model \`{requested}\` for spawn_agent. Available models: {list}`.
- a requested effort must appear in the resolved model's
  `supported_reasoning_levels`, else
  `Reasoning effort \`{effort}\` is not supported for model \`{model}\`. Supported reasoning efforts: {list}`;
  when only a model was requested the model's `default_reasoning_level` applies.

Go parsed `agents.default_subagent_model` /
`default_subagent_reasoning_effort` but never applied them, `exec`'s resolver
required an exact `MultiAgentVersion == target` match (Rust accepts a `v1` or
unversioned preset for a V2 spawn) and never consulted the configured defaults,
and the appserver lane accepted any `args.Model` verbatim (`find_spawn_agent_model_name`
and `validate_spawn_agent_reasoning_effort` had no counterpart).

### Landed

- `model/spawn_agent_models.go` holds the shared rules: `ModelPresetShowInPicker`
  (Rust's List-only predicate, deliberately distinct from Go's looser TUI picker
  helper), `ModelPresetSupportsMultiAgentBackend`, `SpawnAgentModelName` and
  `ValidateSpawnAgentReasoningEffort`, plus `MaxSpawnAgentModelOverrides`.
- `exec` uses those helpers (dropping its private `execContainsString` and the
  exact-version match) and gained `defaultSubagentModel` /
  `defaultSubagentReasoningEffort` fields populated from `agentsConfig`.
- `appserver` gained `modelsManagerForSpawn` (the running agent's catalog, else
  the bundled static catalog), `spawnAgentDefaults` (the invoking turn's
  effective config `[agents]` table) and `resolveSpawnModelOverrides`, called at
  the top of `SpawnAgent` before the child thread is created. Explicit tool
  arguments win over the configured defaults, and both win over the invoking
  step's captured settings from sync91.

### Evidence

- `model.TestSpawnAgentModelNameMatchesRust`: a `v1` and an unversioned preset are
  accepted for the V2 backend, an explicitly `disabled` preset is rejected, the
  available list is picker-visible-only (hidden excluded), and
  `TestSpawnAgentModelNameCapsAvailableModels` pins the five-model cap.
  `TestValidateSpawnAgentReasoningEffortMatchesRust` pins the exact message.
- `exec.TestExecAgentControllerValidatesV2SpawnModelOverrides` now accepts the
  `v1` preset for V2 (Rust's predicate), rejects a model absent from the catalog
  with the available list, rejects an unsupported effort, and adopts the preset's
  default reasoning level; `...AppliesConfiguredSubagentDefaultsLikeRust` covers
  the configured model/effort defaults and an unsupported configured effort.
- `appserver.TestRuntimeAgentControllerResolvesSpawnModelOverridesLikeRust` drives
  a real catalog: the unknown-model error lists the bundled picker-visible models
  (`Unknown model \`missing-model\` ... Available models: gpt-5.6-sol, ...`), a
  model-only request adopts the model and its default level, and an unsupported
  effort produces Rust's exact message; `...SpawnUsesConfiguredSubagentDefaultsLikeRust`
  writes an `[agents]` table into the codex home and asserts the defaults (and
  that an explicit argument still overrides them).
- `TestRuntimeAgentControllerPersistsSpawnMetadataAndGraph` was updated: it
  requested the fake slug `gpt-review`, which Rust also rejects, so it now takes a
  real picker-visible preset from the catalog.
- `gofmt -l` clean on every touched file; `go test ./model/ ./exec/ ./appserver/
  -count=1` green (appserver 133 s); `go test ./... -count=1` green (97 packages)
  on the re-run after one load-sensitive flake (below).

### Recorded observations

- One full-suite run failed with a temp-dir cleanup race in
  `TestRuntimeAgentControllerV2RegistersCanonicalPathAndImplementsTools`
  ("TempDir RemoveAll cleanup: ... The directory is not empty"), which passes in
  isolation (3/3) and passed on the full-suite re-run. For that test
  `resolveSpawnModelOverrides` is a no-op (no explicit model/effort and no invoking
  turn, so there are no configured defaults), so the change cannot be its cause;
  this is the same load-sensitive cleanup class the plan recorded in sync44.
- `go vet ./exec/ ./model/` reports two pre-existing `copylocks` warnings
  (`exec/agent_controller.go` `childRunner := *s.runner` and
  `model/responses_agent.go` `clone := *r`, both present at HEAD and untouched
  here). `go vet ./appserver/` is clean.

### sync92 addendum: the composed tool description has no Go counterpart

Rust also advertises the same catalog inside the spawn_agent *tool description*:
`spawn_agent_models_description(models, version)` filters
`show_in_picker` + `model_supports_multi_agent_backend`, caps at five, and renders
per model

```
- `{slug}`: {description} Reasoning efforts: {a, b (default), c}. Service tiers: {x, y}.
```

under the header `Available model overrides (optional; inherited parent model is
preferred):` (or `No picker-visible model overrides are currently loaded.` when
empty), followed by `SPAWN_AGENT_INHERITED_MODEL_GUIDANCE`
("Spawned agents inherit your current model by default. ..."). For the V2 tool it
is included only when `expose_spawn_agent_model_overrides` is set
(`multi_agents_spec.rs:105`, composed by `spawn_agent_tool_description_v2`).

Go's V2 spawn_agent description is a one-line summary
(`agent/tools_v2.go` "Spawns an agent to work on the specified task. ...") rather
than Rust's composed text, and the inherited-model guidance lives in the usage
hint fragment (`agent.MultiAgentV2ModelOverrideUsageHint`) instead. The plan's
sync74 note already records that divergence ("the v2 `spawn_agent` bundled
description is a Go one-liner rather than Rust's long text"), so adding only the
model list would not reproduce Rust's description; aligning it means porting the
whole composed description (base text, agent-role guidance, usage-hint ordering,
and the catalog list), which stays a lane.

## sync93: rollout compression metrics, scan tolerance and stale temp cleanup (#45461)

Closes the sweep candidate recorded in sync88. The lane turned out to carry
behaviour as well as telemetry: Go's worker had no metrics at all, aborted a pass
when a root could not be scanned, and never removed temp files left behind by an
interrupted pass.

### The divergence

Rust's rollout compression carries three modules the Go worker lacked:

- `compression::metrics`: counters `codex.rollout_compression.{file,run,scan,materialize,temp_cleanup}`,
  duration histograms `file.duration_ms` / `run.duration_ms` / `materialize.duration_ms`,
  and the byte histograms `file.source_bytes` / `file.compressed_bytes` /
  `file.compression_ratio` (basis points, skipped when the source is empty). Run
  metrics carry `trigger`, the failure counters carry `trigger` only for the
  trigger-scoped families, and the completion counter adds
  `completion_reason` (`scan_finished` or `time_budget`), `file_errors`,
  `scan_errors` and `cleanup_errors`.
- `compression::error_metrics` (#45461): every failure counter is labelled with a
  static `stage` and a bounded `error_kind` (fifteen categories), so messages and
  paths never become tags.
- `cleanup_stale_temps`: temp files under the Codex home older than the run
  marker's stale window (6h) are removed before the scan, reporting
  `temp_cleanup "removed"` and tolerating cleanup errors.

The scan itself is also tolerant in Rust: a missing root, an unreadable
directory, an unreadable file type or unreadable session metadata set
`stats.scan_errors` and the pass continues (`skipped_unreadable_meta` for the
metadata cases), where Go returned the error and aborted the whole pass. Rust
tracks `time_budget_exhausted` for the completion reason.

Go also had no global counter/histogram helper: `metrics.Recorder` only exposed
`RecordDuration`, so library code such as `rollout` (which must not import
`telemetry`) could not record counters at all.

### Landed

- `metrics.Recorder` gained `Counter`/`Histogram`, with nil-safe package helpers
  `metrics.Counter`, `metrics.Histogram` and `metrics.RecordDuration` mirroring
  Rust's `codex_otel::global()` calls.
- `rollout/compression_metrics.go` ports the metrics module and the failure
  labels, including the bounded error-kind mapping (errno-specific checks first,
  because Go maps some Windows errnos onto the generic `os.Err` sentinels).
- `rollout/compression.go`: run-level metrics (`skipped_maintenance`,
  `skipped_already_running`, `started`, `completed` with the five completion tags
