## The untriaged queue at 132c2be2 (the sweep re-run)

The sync103 sweep was re-run against the current pin so the next pass starts from
a list instead of a recomputation.

Method: the range `5b1d656018..132c2be239` holds 324 commits with 324 distinct PR
numbers. A PR counts as triaged when its number appears in any tracked `*.md`
(1589 distinct numbers are cited today). That leaves **43 candidates**, listed
below with a theme tag; the tag is a reading of the subject, not a verified
disposition, so every entry still needs the per-PR code check the landed items
got. Two of them were checked here.

### Checked in this pass

- **#45399 "Cancel code mode timer tasks when cleared or the cell finishes" -
  N/A.** Rust replaced one sleeping thread per `setTimeout` with abortable Tokio
  sleep tasks. Go's code mode registers timers with `time.AfterFunc`
  (`tool/code_mode_exec.go:944`), which parks on the runtime timer heap rather
  than a thread or goroutine per timer, `clearTimeout` calls `Stop()`, and the
  timer's delivery already selects on `ctx.Done()` so a completed cell cannot
  emit its output. There is no per-timer resource for the fix to reclaim.
- **#45409 "Add session and originating window IDs to MCP request metadata" -
  gap (portable).** Rust's `with_mcp_tool_call_ids_meta` now writes `sessionId`
  and `windowId` next to `threadId`/`itemId`, and carries a `ToolCallOrigin`
  (item + window) through code-mode waits and compaction. Go's
  `mcpToolCallMetaWithThreadID` (`mcp/api.go:2131`) writes only `threadId`, with
  `callId` added by `augmentToolCallMeta`; `git grep -n sessionId` finds no MCP
  `_meta` writer at all. The `sessionId` half needs the session id plumbed to the
  MCP executor; the `windowId`/`itemId` half additionally needs a Go
  originating-call origin (Go's tool invocations carry no window identity).

### Candidates (theme tag only)

- Guardian structure: #45491 (remove subagent-spawner plumbing), #45492 (split V2
  async scoring), #45493 (deadline helper visibility), #45516 (prompt template
  config), #45518 (reviewers through ThreadManager), #45521 (reviewer startup
  pool), #45537 (reviewer lifecycle into the extension), #45672 (lifecycle
  ownership), #45677 (reporting/denial accounting), #45680 (assessment parsing
  and circuit breaker), #45729 (reviewer config into the extension), #46041
  (approval-test request handling).
- Agent module structure: #45669 (child agent config), #45670 (V2 message
  delivery into `AgentControl`), #45676 (interruption rules into
  `AgentControl`).
- Analytics: #45445 (command/plugin analytics attributed to the invoking model),
  #45535 (tool events classified by call origin), #45649 (elicitation
  classification), #45716 (MCP auth/approval outcomes), #45741 (token history and
  credit formatting helpers), #45742 (account-bound analytics auth).
- Attachments and images: #45499 (local TUI images as portable attachments to
  remote app servers), #45556 (attachment upload/resolution APIs and session
  stores), #46065 (route prepared images through the attachment store).
- Thread/session lifecycle: #45487 (retain persistence acquisition through
  cancellation), #45502 (managed thread lifetimes, cancellation-safe startup),
  #45506 (background persistence for steered user input).
- Daemon and packaging: #45546 (move daemon packages out of the standalone CLI
  installation), #45780 (restore pinned packages to latest stable).
- Memory telemetry: #45956 (storage size after consolidation), #45960 (memory
  version tag).
- Platform and environment: #45533 (share Windows sandbox identity helpers),
  #46293 (skill discovery through `EnvironmentAccess`).
- TUI: #45457 (clipboard routing for tmux/SSH), #46077 (composer stays responsive
  during Command Center session creation).
- Misc: #45496 (global user instruction tracing), #45505 (unified-exec lifecycle
  tracing), #45526 (stage Python runtime wheels from package directories),
  #45755 (canonical plugin disables for shared connectors), #45796
  (`ImageUserInput` in the Python SDK), #46002 (route-aware HTTP request
  execution module).

## sync108: MCP request metadata reports the session, window and originating item (#45409, with #40866)

The first candidate from the queue above to be landed.

### The divergence

Rust's `with_mcp_tool_call_ids_meta` writes `threadId` and `sessionId` on every
MCP request, `windowId` whenever the call has an origin, and `itemId` when that
origin resolved to a Responses item. The origin is `ToolCallOrigin { item_id,
window_id }`, resolved per call from the session (`current_window_id()` =
`{thread_id}:{window_number}`, zero-based) or retained per Code Mode cell. Go's
`_meta` carried only `threadId` (camelCase), `callId` and `_codex_apps`; the
originating item was never reported, and Go's window identity was the hardcoded
string `{thread}:1` at every call site while the window *number* the same
metadata reported was zero-based - an internal inconsistency with Rust's
`{thread_id}:{window_number}` form (Rust's fresh-thread window id is
`test-thread:0`).

### Landed

- `turn.ConversationWindowID` is the single definition of Rust's window identity,
  and `BuildResponsesClientMetadata` derives `x-codex-window-id` (and the turn
  document's `window_id`) from the caller's window number. The app-server turn,
  compaction and web-search metadata, and `codex exec` now all use it, so the
  MCP `windowId` and the request's client metadata cannot disagree.
- `mcp.withMCPToolCallIDsMeta` is Rust's function: `threadId`/`sessionId` from the
  call, `windowId` from the turn's window identity, `itemId` from the call's
  origin, replacing stale values and passing a non-object meta through untouched.
  `MCPToolCallParams` carries the three new values as non-serialized fields, and
  `mcp.ToolExecutorOptions.SessionID`/`WindowID` are fed from
  `turn.ToolRegistryOptions` (the app-server supplies the thread's session id and
  window identity).
- The originating item: `tool.ResponseItem.ID` becomes
  `tool.OriginItemIDContextKey` in `Router.BuildToolCall`, and the MCP executor
  reports it. A nested Code Mode call inherits its cell's origin because the
  delegate clones the parent invocation's context, which is the Go equivalent of
  Rust attaching the cell's retained `ToolCallOrigin`.
- Cell origin retention: the Code Mode executor keeps each cell's origin
  (`cellOrigin`, Rust's `cell_originating_call`) - the Responses item and the
  conversation window the cell started in - recorded the first time the cell is
  observed, and stamps both keys on every nested invocation it dispatches. The
  turn binds its window to the runtime (`CodeModeRuntime.SetTurnWindowID`), and
  the MCP executor prefers a nested call's retained window over the turn's
  (`originWindowID`), so a `wait` - or a compaction that moved the thread to a new
  window - cannot retarget a running cell. The origin is released with the cell
  (`forgetRemoteCell`).
- Window advancement: `compactThreadWithHistory` calls
  `advanceWindowNumber`/`advanceContextWindowID` where it installs the compacted
  history, the point where Rust calls `sess.advance_auto_compact_window()` before
  `replace_compacted_history` (`compact.rs`, `compact_remote_v2.rs`). Go already
  mirrored the new window's delivery-marker resets there, so both
  advance-helpers had no production caller before this pass.
- Window persistence and restore: the same site stamps
  `auto_compact_window_number` and `auto_compact_context_window_id` on the thread
  record, and `restoreConversationWindow` (called when a turn resolves its tool
  router) loads them once per thread, so a thread that resumes in a new process
  keeps reporting the window its last compaction started. A window the live
  process already knows is never overwritten.

### Evidence

- `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterMCPRequestMetadataReportsCallIDsLikeRust` drives a
  real turn against an HTTP MCP server and asserts the wire `tools/call` params:
  the hosted-apps call reports `threadId`, `sessionId` (the thread's session id),
  `windowId` (`{thread}:0`) and `itemId` (the call item's Responses id, not the
  tool call id) next to the existing `callId`. Verified to fail with the origin
  plumbing removed (`_meta[itemId] = <nil>`) and with the session/window
  pass-through removed (`_meta[sessionId] = <nil>`).
- `turn.TestBuildToolCallCarriesOriginItemIDLikeRust` (the dispatched item id
  becomes the invocation's origin, and an item without an id records none),
  `mcp.TestInvocationOriginItemIDLikeRust` (direct, nested-clone and absent
  origins), `mcp.TestMCPToolCallMetaCallIDsLikeRust` (the meta document matrix,
  including stale-value replacement and the non-object passthrough), and
  `turn.TestConversationWindowIDIsZeroBasedLikeRust` (the `{thread}:{n}` form, the
  metadata default, and an explicit window id winning).
- `appserver.TestCompactResponsesClientMetadataCarriesTheTurnDocument` now pins
  `window_id = "thread-1:0"`; it previously pinned the mis-ported `:1`.
- `appserver.TestCompactThreadAdvancesTheConversationWindowLikeRust`: a
  compaction moves the thread from `{thread}:0` to `{thread}:1`, regenerates the
  context-window identity, and the next compaction request's client metadata
  reports the new window id and number. Verified to fail with the advance calls
  removed (`window id after compaction = "thread-window-advance:0"`).
- `tool.TestCodeModeCellRetainsItsOriginLikeRust`: a cell that starts in window 0
  keeps reporting `fc-before`/`thread-1:0` for a nested call issued after the
  thread moved to window 1 and a `wait` resumed the cell, while a second cell
  takes its own origin. Verified to fail with the retention bypassed
  (`nested call 1 origin = "fc-after"/"thread-1:1"`).
- `tool.TestCodeModeForgottenCellDropsItsOrigin` (the retained origin is released
  with the cell), `turn.TestBuildToolRegistryBindsTheTurnWindowToCodeModeCellsLikeRust`
  (the router's `WindowID` reaches the nested invocation through a code-mode host
  double; verified to fail with the binding removed, `nested origin = "fc-turn"/<nil>`),
  and `mcp.TestOriginWindowIDPrefersTheRetainedWindowLikeRust` (a retained window
  wins over the turn's, and a blank override falls back).
- `appserver.TestCompactThreadPersistsAndRestoresTheConversationWindowLikeRust`:
  the compaction stamps window number 1 and the regenerated context-window id on
  the record, a second router over the same store resumes at `{thread}:1` with
  that same context-window id, and a window the process already advanced is not
  overwritten. Verified to fail with the restore disabled (`restored window id =
  "thread-window-persist:0"`).
- `gofmt -l` clean; `go build ./...` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` green
  (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Recorded remainders in this lane

- **First-observation origin.** Go records a cell's origin lazily, on the first
  nested call the delegate routes, because that is the first point where both the
  cell id and the invoking invocation are known. Rust records it when the cell is
  created, so a cell whose very first nested call arrived without an identifiable
  parent would keep an item-less origin in Go. The window is still retained, and
  in the transports Go implements the delegate always runs inside the cell's
  owning exec/wait invocation.
- **Persistence medium.** Rust stamps the pair on the compacted rollout item
  (`CompactedHistoryMetadata { window_number, window_ids }`), which travels with
  the history and is restored by the history load path. Go stamps the same pair
  on the thread record's metadata instead, because Go's compacted history item is
  a `contextCompaction` session item with no window fields and the record is what
  every resume path already loads. The observable behaviour (a resumed thread
  keeps its window) is the same; a hand-written rollout that carries the Rust item
  fields but no record metadata would not be restored by Go.
- **Go-only `_meta.thread_id`.** Go's MCP request `_meta` also carries a
  snake_case `thread_id` (`mcp/tool_executor.go`'s `requestMetaForCall`) that
  Rust has never sent - Rust has used camelCase `threadId` since #18093. It is
  left in place here (removing it changes an unrelated wire field) and recorded
  for a dedicated sweep of the whole `_meta` document.

## sync112: command and plugin-measurement analytics name the invoking model (#45445)

The second candidate from the queue above to be landed.

### The divergence

Rust's tool-item event base carries no model attribution by default, but #45445
added `model_slug` and `reasoning_effort` to the command-execution event and to
plugin measurement events, filled from the *resolved step settings of the step
that invoked the command*: the analytics reducer stores the model context
alongside a tool item's start timestamp, keeps the **first** context when a
repeated start notification arrives, and hands it to the event builder at
completion. That is what makes the attribution correct when the model changes
while a background process is still running.

Go's tool events had no model fields at all, and the command-execution event was
built at completion from the item plus the turn's *current* run config, so a
model switch mid-command would have been attributed to the new model.

### Landed

- `telemetry.CodexCommandExecutionEventParams` gained `ModelSlug` and
  `ReasoningEffort` (JSON `model_slug` / `reasoning_effort`, `null` when absent,
  like Rust's `Option` fields), and
  `CodexPluginMeasurementsInput`/`CodexPluginMeasurementEventParams` gained the
  same two labels.
- `appserver` carries the context: `rememberToolItemModelContext` stores the
  resolved settings for a `(thread, turn, item)` where the command execution
  *starts* (the dispatcher's start notification and the unified-exec begin), the
  first context wins, and `emitCommandExecutionAnalyticsEvent` consumes it. When
  no start was observed the emitter falls back to the completion-time settings,
  which is the Go equivalent of Rust's carried context for the paths Go models.
- `tool.ShellExecutorOptions.ModelContext` reports the invoking model; the shell
  executor reads it once per command, **before** execution, and stamps it onto the
  `plugin.PluginMeasurementBatch`, which `RuntimeRouter.emitPluginMeasurements`
  (extracted from the tracker closure) forwards to the event. The app-server
  supplies the provider from the active turn's resolved settings.

### Evidence

- `telemetry.TestCodexCommandExecutionEventSerializesExpectedRustShape` pins the
  `null` labels next to Rust's expected shape and then a labelled event
  (`invoking-model`/`max`).
- `appserver.TestCommandExecutionAnalyticsUsesTheInvokingModelLikeRust` mirrors
  Rust's reducer test: a recorded `invoking-model`/`max` survives a repeated start
  notification and wins over the completion-time run config `later-model`/`low`;
  a command whose start was not observed falls back to the completion-time model.
  Verified to fail with the carried context ignored (`model_slug = later-model`).
- `tool.TestShellExecutorPluginMeasurementsCarryTheInvokingModelLikeRust` drives a
  real measurement batch (fake runner writes the sidecar envelope the trusted
  script would produce) while the model switches mid-command: the batch keeps
  `invoking-model`, and a command without a provider reports absent labels.
  Verified to fail with the capture moved after execution
  (`measurement attribution = "later-model"/"max"`).
- `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterCommandExecutionEmitsAnalyticsLikeRust` now asserts
  the end-to-end event carries `model_slug = "gpt-5"`, the turn's resolved model.
- `appserver.TestPluginMeasurementEventCarriesTheInvokingModelLikeRust` covers the
  emission wiring for labelled and unlabelled batches (the recording sink's
  `pluginMeasurements` channel is now initialized, which it previously was not).
- `gofmt` clean on every touched file; `go test ./... -count=1` green
  (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Recorded differences in this lane

- Rust's reducer drops a tool item event entirely when no start fact was recorded
  (`let Some(..) else { warn; return }`); Go emits from the item and falls back to
  the completion-time settings. Go's start notifications cover the surfaces it
  models, so the observable difference is limited to items synthesized without a
  start.
- Rust captures the context in core (`ExecCommandBegin`/`ExecApprovalRequest`) and
  carries it through approval and Guardian review; Go resolves it from the active
  turn's run config at command start, which is the same resolved step settings for
  the transports Go implements.

### Next queue candidate: #45535 (`tool_event_type`), site-checked

While landing #45445 the neighbouring commit in the same event base was checked:

- Rust's `CodexToolItemEventBase` gained `tool_event_type`
  (`model_tool_call` | `inner_tool_call`), serialized as `null` when the origin
  evidence is missing or conflicting at emission time. The classification lives
  in the analytics reducer and uses **exact call-ID evidence** only: a call ID
  seen in a sampled model response is a model tool call, a call ID dispatched as a
  child of an executing tool is an inner tool call, and later evidence never
  revises an emitted event. It explicitly does not infer origin from cell
  association or parent lineage.
- Go's `telemetry.CodexToolItemEventBase` has no such field, and every Go tool
  event is built from the app-server's thread item at completion. The sampled
  call IDs are derivable from the turn's model response items, which the
  app-server already handles; the child-call set needs the Code Mode delegate's
  nested runtime call IDs (`tool/code_mode_exec.go`). Whether Go emits tool items
  (and therefore events) for nested calls at all is still unverified here - if it
  does not, the `inner_tool_call` class has no producer and only the
  `model_tool_call`/`null` half is portable, with the rest belonging to the
  #36729 correlation lane.

## sync113: tool events name their immediate initiator (#45535)

The queue candidate that the #45445 site check opened.

### The divergence

Rust's `CodexToolItemEventBase` gained `tool_event_type`
(`model_tool_call` | `inner_tool_call`, serialized `null` when the evidence is
missing or conflicting). `enrich_tool_response_event` classifies it from exactly
two per-`(thread, turn)` maps, and explicitly not from cell association or parent
lineage:

- `response_ids_by_call_id`, published by `ingest_sampling_response_completed`
  from the tool call ids of one sampled response. Core collects those ids at
  `ResponseEvent::OutputItemDone` (function/custom tool calls by call id;
  tool-search/local-shell calls by their optional call id; web-search and
  image-generation calls by item id, capped per response) and takes the vector at
  `ResponseEvent::Completed`.
- `cell_ids_by_child_call_id`, published by the code-mode child-call facts.

A call id in the sampled set only is a model tool call, in the child set only an
inner tool call, and anything else - including both - is left unclassified. Go's
tool-item base had no such field.

### Landed

- `telemetry.CodexToolItemEventBase.ToolEventType` plus
  `ToolEventTypeModelToolCall`/`ToolEventTypeInnerToolCall`, so every tool event
  (command execution, file change, MCP, dynamic, collab, web search, image
  generation) reports the class.
- `appserver` keeps per-`(thread, turn)` `sampledToolCalls` (call id -> response
  id, bounded at Rust's 256) and `codeModeChildCalls` (call id -> cell id), drops
  both when the turn closes, and `toolEventTypeForCall` implements Rust's
  three-way rule including the conflicting-evidence case.
- The sampled call ids are collected the way Rust collects them: from the
  streaming `OutputItemDone` items (same item-type match, bounded per response)
  and published with the response id at `Completed`; `result.ModelResponses()`
  publishes the same evidence at turn finalization, which covers agents that do
  not stream (Rust's core event loop sees every response; Go's non-streaming
  agents only expose the aggregated result).
- Child call ids come from a new `CodeModeRuntime.SetNestedCallObserver`, which
  reports every dispatched nested call with its cell from both the remote
  delegate and the in-process dispatch. The app-server records it against the
  thread's active turn, which is the turn whose cell dispatched the call.
- Every emitter sets the field: the shared `toolItemAnalyticsBase` (collab, web
  search, image generation) and the inline command-execution, file-change, MCP and
  dynamic-tool bases.

### Evidence

- `telemetry.TestCodexCommandExecutionEventSerializesExpectedRustShape` pins
  `tool_event_type: null` in the base shape and a `model_tool_call` event;
  `TestCodexFileChangeEventSerializesExpectedRustShape` pins the null base field.
- `appserver.TestToolEventTypeRequiresExactSampledCallEvidenceLikeRust`: no
  evidence -> null; sampled call ids -> `model_tool_call` (per turn, so another
  turn sees nothing); a closed turn drops its evidence; blank ids are never
  recorded.
- `appserver.TestToolEventTypeClassifiesCodeModeChildCallsLikeRust`: a dispatched
  child call -> `inner_tool_call`, the same call id appearing in both sets ->
  null, and a child call without an active turn is not attributed.
- `tool.TestCodeModeNestedCallsAreObservedLikeRust`: a nested remote dispatch is
  reported with its cell and call id.
- `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterCommandExecutionEmitsAnalyticsLikeRust` and
  `TestRuntimeRouterMCPToolCallEmitsAnalyticsLikeRust` now assert
  `model_tool_call` on real turns; the command-execution assertion was verified to
  fail (`tool_event_type = (*string)(nil)`) with the sampled-call publication
  removed.
- `gofmt` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` green (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Recorded remainders in this lane

- Go emits an event for a nested call only when that call produces a thread item
  of its own - in practice a nested command through unified exec. Those now
  classify as `inner_tool_call`; Rust additionally emits dynamic tool-call events
  for code-mode `exec`/`wait` and for every child call and carries `cell_id`,
  `parent_call_id` and the originating/subsequent response ids on all tool events
  (the #36729 correlation lane), which Go still lacks. The child-call set landed
  here is the evidence that lane needs.
- The conflicting-evidence case is implemented and tested, but only a call id that
  is both a sampled model call and a cell dispatch can produce it; with the
  current Go event coverage that combination does not occur in production.

## sync114: tool events carry the Code Mode and response correlation fields (#36729)

The correlation lane the #45535 landing unblocked: the evidence maps for child
calls and sampled responses already existed, so this pass adds the fields Rust's
`enrich_tool_response_event` fills from them.

### The divergence

Rust's `CodexToolItemEventBase` carries `session_id`, `cell_id`,
`parent_call_id`, `originating_response_id` and `subsequent_response_id`. The
first four are filled when an event is recorded, from exactly three sources:

- `session_id` is the thread metadata's session identity (reported on every tool
  event);
- `cell_id` comes from `cell_ids_by_child_call_id`, so a child call names the cell
  it ran in;
- `parent_call_id` comes from the cell's `parent_call_id`, and only when it
  differs from the event's own item id;
- `originating_response_id` is the call's own sampled response, falling back to
  the cell's originating response for a child call;
- an event whose cell is unknown loses both `cell_id` and `parent_call_id`, and a
  cell's own call (the exec/wait call) is not a child call, so it carries no cell.

`subsequent_response_id` is different in kind: Rust buffers every correlated tool
event (`ToolEventEmission::ImmediateUnlessCorrelated` for item notifications,
`AwaitResponse` for the code-mode facts) and releases it when a *later* sampled
response completes, stamping that response as the subsequent one; the buffer is
bounded (256, oldest emitted first) and flushed on turn completion and thread
close. Go's base had none of these fields.

### Landed

- The five base fields (`session_id`, `cell_id`, `parent_call_id`,
  `originating_response_id`, `subsequent_response_id`), so every tool event
  reports them.
- `CodeModeRuntime.SetCallObserver` replaced `SetNestedCallObserver` and reports
  Rust's `CodeModeToolCallFact` stream: `cell_started` with the exec call that
  created the cell (both the in-process and remote exec paths), `child_started`
  from both nested dispatch paths, and `cell_closed` when a cell finishes
  (in-process completion and `forgetRemoteCell`).
- `appserver.observeCodeModeCall` records those facts: cells per thread with their
  parent call and originating response (filled at start and re-checked whenever a
  sampled response publishes, mirroring `ingest_sampling_response_completed`),
  child calls per turn (only for a cell that is known, as Rust requires), and the
  turn that closed a cell so `forgetSampledToolCalls` can drop it at turn end.
- `enrichToolEventBase` implements Rust's `enrich_tool_response_event` (including
  the unknown-cell clearing and the parent-equals-item suppression) and is called
  by all tool-event emitters, which also now report `session_id`.

### Evidence

- `appserver.TestEnrichToolEventBaseCorrelatesCodeModeCallsLikeRust`: a child call
  gets its cell from the child evidence, its parent call from the cell (suppressed
  for the cell's own call), its originating response from its own sampled response
  or the cell's fallback, keeps `subsequent_response_id` absent, and an orphan
  child call keeps neither field; after the cell closes and the turn ends, the
  correlation is gone.
- `tool.TestCodeModeCallObservationsFollowRustFacts`: the observation stream is
  exactly `cell_started(parent=exec call)` -> `child_started(cell, call)` ->
  `cell_closed(cell)`; `TestCodeModeNestedCallsAreObservedLikeRust` keeps the
  child-call case.
- `telemetry` shape tests pin `session_id` (with Rust's `session-thread-1`
  value) and the four null correlation fields on the command-execution and
  file-change events; `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterCommandExecutionEmitsAnalyticsLikeRust`
  and `TestRuntimeRouterMCPToolCallEmitsAnalyticsLikeRust` assert the end-to-end
  `session_id`.
- `gofmt` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` green (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Recorded remainders in this lane

- **Deferred emission and `subsequent_response_id`.** Landed in sync115 below.
- **Code-mode `exec`/`wait` dynamic events.** Rust also emits a dynamic tool-call
  event for every code-mode `exec`/`wait` completion (from the `Completed`
  fact, with the cell id set explicitly). Go emits no dynamic tool-call event for
  those calls unless they also produce a thread item, so that half of #36729 is
  still missing; the cell and parent evidence it needs is now in place.

## sync115: correlated tool events wait for the next sampled response (#36729 remainder)

The delivery half of the correlation lane.

### The divergence

Rust does not publish a correlated tool event the moment it is built. Its
reducer records every tool event through `record_tool_event`: an event with
neither a cell nor an originating response is published immediately, while a
correlated one is appended to the turn's `pending_tool_events` (bounded at
`MAX_TOOL_RESPONSE_ENTRIES`, oldest published first when the queue overflows).
The queue is released when a *later* sampled response completes: each waiting
event is re-enriched from the updated state, and an event whose originating
response differs from the freshly completed one is published with that response
as its `subsequent_response_id`; everything else keeps waiting. The rest is
flushed on turn completion (`flush_pending_tool_events`) and on thread close, and
a final `flush()` drains whatever is left when the analytics queue closes.

Go published every tool event immediately, so `subsequent_response_id` could
never be filled.

### Landed

- `RuntimeRouter.emitToolEvent` is the funnel every tool-event emitter now goes
  through: it publishes an uncorrelated event immediately and queues a correlated
  one in the per-`(thread, turn)` `pendingToolEvents` (bounded at 256, oldest
  emitted first on overflow), holding the event's own base pointer so the release
  can still fill it.
- `releasePendingToolEvents` runs whenever a sampled response completes (the
  stream handler's `Completed` case and the turn-result publisher both call
  `rememberSampledToolCalls`), re-enriches each waiting event from the state, and
  publishes the ones whose originating response is a different, known response
  with `subsequent_response_id` set to it. Events whose originating response is
  the one that just completed - or unknown - keep waiting, exactly like Rust's
  release loop.
- `flushPendingToolEvents` runs where a turn closes (before the turn's evidence
  is dropped) and `flushThreadPendingToolEvents` plus `forgetThreadToolEvidence`
  run on thread close, so a closing turn or thread cannot lose or leak events.

### Evidence

- `appserver.TestCorrelatedToolEventsWaitForTheNextResponseLikeRust`: a correlated
  event is not published on its own response, is released with
  `subsequent_response_id` by the next one, keeps waiting when the response that
  completes is its own, an uncorrelated event publishes immediately, and the
  turn-close flush publishes what is left.
- `appserver.TestPendingToolEventQueueIsBoundedLikeRust`: the overflow publishes
  the oldest event.
- `appserver.TestStreamHandlerPublishesSampledResponsesAndReleasesEventsLikeRust`:
  drive the real stream handler - `OutputDone` collects the call id, `Completed`
  publishes it (so the call classifies as a model tool call) and releases the
  waiting event with that response as the subsequent one.
- `appserver.TestClosingThreadFlushesAndDropsToolEvidenceLikeRust`: closing a
  thread publishes its waiting events and drops its cell and per-turn evidence.
- The whole appserver suite (including every end-to-end tool-event test) passes
  unchanged with the deferral active, which is the check that the turn-close flush
  keeps delivery working for turns that do not sample another response.
- Verified falsifiable: short-circuiting `emitToolEvent` to publish correlated
  events immediately fails all three deferral tests
  (`a correlated event must wait, emitted = 1`).
- `gofmt` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` green (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Remaining in this lane

- **Code-mode `exec`/`wait` dynamic events.** Rust publishes a dynamic tool-call
  event for every code-mode `exec`/`wait` completion from its `Completed` fact
  (cell id set explicitly, buffered as `AwaitResponse`). Go emits a dynamic
  tool-call event only when a call produces a `dynamicToolCall` thread item, so
  code-mode calls still produce no tool analytics of their own. The cell, parent
  and response evidence they need is now in place, so this is the last piece of
  #36729.

## sync116: code-mode exec and wait calls report dynamic tool-call events (#36729 final piece)

The last piece of the correlation lane: the events the facts exist for.

### The divergence

Rust wraps every code-mode `exec`/`wait` handler in a `CodeModeToolCallGuard` that
publishes `CodeModeToolCallFact::Completed` when the handler returns - the call id,
the cell the call ran in (set once the cell starts, or from the resumed cell for a
wait), the tool name, the observed window, and the terminal status (`Interrupted`
by default, `Completed`/`Failed` through `finish(success)`). The reducer turns each
fact into a **dynamic tool-call event**: the cell id is set explicitly from the
fact (not derived), `dynamic_tool_name` is the `exec`/`wait` tool name, `success`
is true only for `Completed`, the review summary defaults (zero counts, unknown
approval outcome), `duration_ms` and `execution_duration_ms` are the same observed
window, and the event is buffered like every other correlated tool event.

Go emitted a dynamic tool-call event only when a call produced a
`dynamicToolCall` thread item, so code-mode calls produced no tool analytics at
all.

### Landed

- `tool.CodeModeCallObservation` gained the `completed` kind carrying the fact's
  fields, and `codeModeCallStatusFor` classifies a finished call (cancelled ->
  interrupted, error or unsuccessful output -> failed, otherwise completed).
- `codeModeExecExecutor.Execute` now wraps its body (`execute`) so every return
  path publishes the completion fact with the cell the call ran in - the remote
  path reports the host's cell id, the in-process path the cell it created - and
  `codeModeWaitExecutor.Execute` does the same for a resumed cell with the `wait`
  tool name.
- `appserver.emitCodeModeToolCallEvent` builds the dynamic tool-call event from the
  fact, mirroring Rust's construction and defaults, classifies it
  (`tool_event_type`), enriches it (parent call, originating response) and queues
  it through `emitToolEvent`, so a code-mode call cannot be published before the
  response that follows it is known.

### Evidence

- `tool.TestCodeModeCallsPublishCompletionFactsLikeRust`: a remote exec that yields
  and a wait that completes it both publish a completion fact with their own tool
  name, the shared cell id, `completed` status and a valid observed window.
- `tool.TestInProcessCodeModeExecPublishesCompletionFactLikeRust`: the in-process
  yielding path reports its cell start with the exec call as parent and a
  completion fact carrying that same cell.
- `tool.TestCodeModeCallObservationsFollowRustFacts` now pins the whole fact
  stream: `cell_started(parent=exec)` -> `completed(exec, cell)` ->
  `child_started(cell, call)` -> `cell_closed(cell)`.
- `appserver.TestCodeModeToolCallEventFollowsRustFacts`: a completed exec fact
  yields an event with the cell, no parent (the cell's own call), the originating
  response, `model_tool_call`, `completed` status, the observed window as both
  durations, and the session id; a failed wait fact yields the `wait` tool name,
  `failed` status with a `tool_error` kind, and the exec call as its parent.
  Verified to fail with the observation wiring removed
  (`timed out waiting for codex_dynamic_tool_call_event`).
- `gofmt` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` green (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Recorded observation

- Go's inline in-process path (a script without `yield_control` and without an
  exec pragma) returns through `executeScript` without creating a cell, so its
  completion fact carries no cell id where Rust always starts a cell and reports
  one. The call event is still published; only the cell attribution is absent for
  that path.

## sync117: MCP tool calls report the connector and their elicitation classification (#45649/#45716, with #32867)

The next queue item.

### The divergence

Rust's `CodexMcpToolCallEventParams` carry the trusted `connector_id` the call
resolved to (#32867) and an `elicitation_type` (#45649):

- `auth_or_link` when the raw result carries trusted connector authentication
  failure metadata, captured *before* result callbacks or model-facing rewrites
  can change it (#45716);
- `approval` when a dispatched approval request is denied, times out or aborts,
  covering both MCP elicitation and legacy user-input approvals;
- null for an ordinary call.

Producers enqueue the classification on the analytics queue *before* the item
completes; the reducer keeps the first classification per thread/turn/item in a
queue bounded at 256 (oldest evicted when full), consumes it when the
`McpToolCall` item completes, and drops a closing thread's entries. Go's MCP event
had neither field and no classification at all.

### Landed

- `telemetry.CodexMCPToolCallEventParams` gained `ConnectorID` and
  `ElicitationType` (`connector_id` / `elicitation_type`, null when absent) plus
  `ElicitationTypeAuthOrLink`/`ElicitationTypeApproval`.
- `appserver.rememberMCPToolCallElicitation` / `takeMCPToolCallElicitation` /
  `forgetThreadMCPToolCallElicitations` implement the bounded queue with Rust's
  semantics: the first classification for a call wins, taking consumes it,
  entries are keyed by thread/turn/call id, the oldest is evicted at the bound,
  and closing a thread drops only its own entries.
- The auth-failure producer is
  `ToolExecutorOptions.ConnectorAuthFailureObserver`, invoked by the MCP executor
  when the raw apps result passes `IsConnectorAuthFailureFromToolResult` - before
  `maybeRequestCodexAppsAuthElicitation` can rewrite it, and independently of
  whether the auth-elicitation feature is enabled, which is Rust's behaviour. The
  registry and turn options carry the observer and the app-server records
  `auth_or_link`.
- The approval producer wraps `ApproveMCPToolCall`: a denied, aborted or failed
  approval queues `approval`, an approval queues nothing.
- `emitMCPToolCallAnalyticsEvent` reports the connector id from the item data and
  consumes the classification.

### Evidence

- `telemetry.TestCodexMCPToolCallEventSerializesExpectedRustShape` pins null
  `connector_id`/`elicitation_type` on an ordinary call and a classified event
  with both values.
- `appserver.TestMCPToolCallElicitationQueueLikeRust`: first-wins, single
  consumption, no cross-thread/turn/call leakage, oldest-first eviction at the
  bound, and per-thread clearing on close.
- `appserver.TestMCPToolCallAnalyticsReportsElicitationClassificationLikeRust`:
  a queued `auth_or_link` reaches the emitted event with the connector, while an
  ordinary call reports null for both and cannot reuse the classification.
  Verified to fail with the consumption removed
  (`elicitation type = (*string)(nil)`).
- `appserver.TestMCPToolCallApprovalClassificationLikeRust`: decline and cancel
  queue `approval`; accept does not.
- `mcp.TestMCPToolExecutorReportsConnectorAuthFailuresLikeRust`: an apps result
  with connector auth-failure metadata is reported once with its call id, and an
  ordinary result is not reported.
- `appserver.TestRuntimeRouterMCPToolCallEmitsAnalyticsLikeRust` now asserts an
  ordinary end-to-end MCP call carries neither field.
- `gofmt` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` green (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Newly recorded lane: the app analytics event family

Rust's #45649/#45716 half also propagates the classification to **app-usage**
analytics: `CustomAnalyticsFact::AppUsed` carries an `elicitation_type`, and the
`codex_app_used` event (like `codex_app_mentioned`) is emitted with
`CodexAppMetadata` plus that classification. Go has no app analytics event family
at all - `git grep -n "codex_app_used\|AppUsed\|CodexAppMetadata"` matches no Go
file, and the telemetry event-type inventory has no `codex_app_*` entry - so that
half has no landing site until the two app events (and their metadata extraction
from the MCP app calls) are ported. Recorded as the next item in this lane.

## sync118: the app analytics event family (#45716's app half, with #45649's classification)

The lane sync117 recorded: Rust's app-usage half of the elicitation classification needed two
events Go did not have at all.

### The divergence

Rust reports two app events, both built from `CodexAppMetadata` (connector id, thread, turn,
app name, product client id, invoke type, model slug):

- `codex_app_mentioned` for every connector the turn input named explicitly (the same parse that
  feeds the session's connector selection), with `invoke_type = explicit`;
- `codex_app_used` for every host-owned apps call, with `invoke_type = explicit` when the
  connector is in the session's selection and `implicit` otherwise, the turn's voice session id,
  and the elicitation classification the call carried.

The mention parse also **merges the mentioned connectors into the session's connector selection**
(`SessionState::merge_connector_selection`), and app usage is deduplicated per `(turn,
connector)` - a call without a connector is always reported, the key set is bounded at
`ANALYTICS_EVENT_DEDUPE_MAX_KEYS = 4096` and cleared when full, and the first event for a key
keeps its classification.

Go had no `codex_app_*` event at all, and its modelled `state.SessionState` connector selection
had no production caller, so `invoke_type` had no source either.

### Landed

- `telemetry.CodexAppMetadata` plus `CodexAppMentionedEventRequest` / `CodexAppUsedEventParams` /
  `CodexAppUsedEventRequest`, the `AppEventSink`, the client and exporter methods, and the
  `explicit`/`implicit` invocation classes.
- `appserver.rememberExplicitAppMentions` merges the turn's explicit app ids into the thread's
  session state (a new per-thread `state.SessionState` owned by the router) and emits one mention
  event per connector with its catalog name, the product client id and the turn's model. It runs
  where the turn's input items are assembled, which now receives the connection id.
- `appserver.emitCodexAppUsedEvent` implements Rust's dedup (per turn and connector, bounded, first
  event wins), reports `invoke_type` from the thread's selection, and passes the classification
  through. The MCP tool-call emitter calls it for host-owned apps calls, so the classification
  sync117 queued reaches both events.
- Closing a thread drops its session state.

### Evidence

- `telemetry.TestCodexAppEventsSerializeExpectedRustShape`: both events' flattened metadata, the
  used event's null voice session and classification, and an ordinary call's nulls.
- `appserver.TestExplicitAppMentionsPopulateTheSelectionLikeRust`: an `app://` mention emits the
  mention event (connector, explicit invoke type, thread/turn, model) and lands in the session
  selection, while a `plugin://` mention is not treated as an app.
- `appserver.TestCodexAppUsedEventFollowsRustDedupAndSelection`: the first event keeps its
  classification, the same turn and connector is deduplicated, another connector and another turn
  are not, a call without a connector is always reported, and a selected connector reports
  `explicit`.
- `appserver.TestMCPToolCallAnalyticsReportsElicitationClassificationLikeRust` now asserts the
  apps call's `codex_app_used` event carries the connector, app name and the same classification.
  Verified to fail with the app-used emission removed (`timed out waiting for codex_app_used`).
- `gofmt` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` green (97 packages, no `FAIL`).

### Recorded remainders in this lane

- `voice_session_id` stays null: Rust reads it from the turn's voice session, and Go does not model
  a voice session identity on a turn at all (the realtime package only has a replay fixture).
- Rust also collects connector ids from **skill** items
  (`collect_explicit_app_ids_from_skill_items`) before merging the selection. Go's mention
  collection covers user-input mentions (`plugin.CollectExplicitAppIDs`), so a connector named
  only through a skill would be missing from the selection.
